# Finish HCU remake M2, M3, and M4

**Current product-scope decision (2026-10-21; supersedes campaign requirements in older plan text):** This port is multiplayer-only. Campaign missions and campaign-only maps are out of scope. The intended modes include stock multiplayer, co-op, and AI bots; those modes remain in scope and must retain the CE AI/game-rule behavior they require. M2 Steps 7–8 therefore qualify the 13 stock multiplayer maps (starting with Longest and Blood Gulch), plus the UI bootstrap map. Campaign-map footprint rows in historical 24-map reports are diagnostic context only and are not capacity exit criteria. Do not spend time building a campaign fit design. Do not interpret this scope change as permission to omit multiplayer maps, disable bots, or stub co-op/game rules. See the governing scope amendment in [plans/5.hcu-remake.md](5.hcu-remake.md).

See [`m2-hs-stack-alignment-fix-review.md`](../docs/reports/m2-hs-stack-alignment-fix-review.md), [`m2-next-capacity-design-review.md`](../docs/reports/m2-next-capacity-design-review.md), [`m2-ce-lifecycle-path-audit.md`](../docs/reports/m2-ce-lifecycle-path-audit.md), and [`m2-streamed-ctag-destination-design.md`](../docs/reports/m2-streamed-ctag-destination-design.md). Halo-64 remains a comparison reference; its N64 ABI, pools, and feature cuts are not PS2 changes to copy.

## Summary

Load a real CE tag image through the parent HP2 path, prove it fits the budget-v2 arenas, then run CE player movement and collision from that image, then replace the parent M13 combat path with CE weapons. [plans/5.hcu-remake.md](5.hcu-remake.md) is the acceptance authority. This file is the operational plan for remake M2–M4 only.

## Instructions

Update this file as work proceeds. After each numbered step, edit that step in place. Set `Status` to `done` or `partial`, or leave it `not done`, and name the ELF hash, command, test count, or log that proves the new state. Set a milestone’s status to done only when every step in it is done and the exit sentence in [plans/5.hcu-remake.md](5.hcu-remake.md) for that milestone is true. A finished subagent group does not finish its milestone.

Update the M2, M3, and M4 rows in the milestone table of [plans/5.hcu-remake.md](5.hcu-remake.md) only when that exit is met. Do not rewrite historical paragraphs there.

Number these milestones Remake M2, M3, and M4. The Desktop plan at `/home/ai/Desktop/HCU plan` used “Milestone 4” for M1 startup interfaces, and that work is already done. Do not conflate the two numbering schemes.

### Which plan depends on which

1. [plans/5.hcu-remake.md](5.hcu-remake.md) governs architecture, ownership, budget version 3, and the exit gates. A step here cannot weaken those gates.
2. This file is the execution plan for remake M2, M3, and M4. The Desktop plan closed remake M0 and M1 and is not a second acceptance source for these milestones.
3. [docs/investigations/final_milestone_plan.md](../docs/investigations/final_milestone_plan.md) and [docs/hcu-tag-loader-contract.md](../docs/hcu-tag-loader-contract.md) sharpen tests and name gaps. They do not close a gate.
4. The other notes in [docs/investigations/](../docs/investigations/) and the JSON in [docs/reports/](../docs/reports/) are supplemental. Reports pinned to closure ELF `a4b332b7…`, `b3f59aa6…`, or `81208465…` are historical until regenerated against the current closure. [docs/investigations/m2_loader_failure_auditor.md](../docs/investigations/m2_loader_failure_auditor.md) still describes the PS2 file layer as a placeholder. M1 replaced that for `host:/`. Do not reopen M1 to satisfy that sentence.

Dependency order is the completed M1 host boundary, then M2’s tag image and arenas, then M3’s tick, collision, and snapshot, then M4’s combat. A later milestone may prepare fixtures early. Its runtime exit cannot be claimed before the upstream owner exists.

### Ownership and budget

HCU `source/` owns simulation. Modified HCU `port/ps2` owns Xbox replacements. halo-ps2 owns GS/VU1/DMA, pad, timer, HP2, and boot arenas. Parent `src/game` stays a comparison fixture until remake M6. Do not grow it.

Budget version 3 records the user-approved 262,144-byte transfer from reserve to `elf_static`. Keep the remaining 5,505,024-byte reserve untouched; no additional reserve use is authorized. Campaign maps are explicitly out of scope under the product decision above and are not to be counted as a requirement or as a permitted tradeoff. Budget version 3 in [config/memory-budget.json](../config/memory-budget.json) commits 27,787,264 bytes and leaves that reserve unspent, for 33,554,432 bytes total. `resident_gameplay` is 6,815,744 bytes. `current_bsp` is 6,291,456 bytes. `dynamic_simulation` is 2,621,440 bytes. `frame_scratch` is 393,216 bytes. `streaming` is 1,572,864 bytes. GS eDRAM, IOP RAM, and SPU2 RAM are separate stores.

### Evidence

Tie every guest claim to the exact ELF hash, source pins, PCSX2 mode, settings hash, and log. A 5-byte `maps/ui.map`, a BIOS disc prompt, or `m1-ui-map-request` is not `m2-ui-map-loaded`. Do not invent a draw path. PCSX2 evidence is not a physical-console pass. Older guest success does not qualify a newly linked ELF.

Preserve the dirty worktree. Stage exact paths. Commit only when a milestone’s exit is true and the user asks.

### Subagent waves

Groups in one wave have disjoint write sets and run together. Only the coordinating agent edits this file, [plans/5.hcu-remake.md](5.hcu-remake.md), and [src/stream/hcu_main.c](../src/stream/hcu_main.c). Subagents return evidence and do not mark a milestone done.

| Wave | Groups | May start when |
|---|---|---|
| M2 wave 1 | A, B, C, D | Now. Write sets do not overlap. |
| M2 wave 2 | Integrator | A–D have returned. Owns `hcu_main.c` and the guest rerun. |
| M3 fixtures | E, F, G | During M2 wave 1. Their write sets do not include M2 files. Runtime exit waits on M2 BSP residency. |
| M3 exit | Integrator | M2 exit is true and E–G traces match. |
| M4 fixtures | H, I | During M3 fixture work. Do not edit weapon simulation or `src/game`. |
| M4 exit | J | M3 collision owner is the production path and H–I traces exist. |

Data ISO `build/hcu-m1-disc-data.iso` (SHA-256 `79f2855a5953483637ca060e778589a9f8c54e5fc4cda4a69347f22484457d79`) contains `MAPS/UI.HP2` (282,624 bytes) and `MAPS/UI.MAP` (33,582,080 bytes). The optical-profile ELF stops at `validate-file` for `cdrom:/maps/ui.map`. That is not an optical read, and it is not this file’s exit.

The registered map service accepts only the UI leaf. Any other name, including a campaign map, fails `map_leaf` before a package is opened.

```54:63:src/stream/hcu_map_service.c
static int map_leaf(const char *map_name, char leaf[16])
{
	static const char ui_path[] = "levels\\ui\\ui";
	static const char ui_path_slash[] = "levels/ui/ui";
	memset(leaf, 0, 16);
	if (!map_name || (strcmp(map_name, ui_path) != 0 &&
		strcmp(map_name, ui_path_slash) != 0 && strcmp(map_name, "ui") != 0))
		return 0;
	memcpy(leaf, "ui", 3);
	return 1;
}
```

## Remake M2 — Fixed memory and a runtime tag image

**Milestone status: in progress; exit not met.** Step 1 and the static part of step 3 have current evidence. The current production-profile ELF reaches `m2-ui-map-loaded` in PCSX2 interpreter and recompiler; a separate diagnostic profile completes two CE unpublish → parent-owner-release → UI reload cycles in both modes. The native ILP32 CE/parent fixture exercises malformed CTAG rejection, real schema rejection before publication, initial-BSP post-write failure, and package-backed BSP rollback/retry. Step 8 residency, Step 9 multiplayer map transaction coverage, and Step 10 multiplayer-map guest acceptance remain open. UI load is not multiplayer-map load or visible rendering. Under the scope amendment above, only the 13 multiplayer maps gate M2; campaign-only maps do not.

**Exit:** map load/unload returns to a stable arena baseline; no steady-state heap allocation; measured high-water fits each assigned EE region and preserves the reserve; GS, IOP, and SPU2 bytes are reported in their own stores. Guest acceptance is a current-hash `--stage m2-ui` plus one gameplay-map load after the capacity design is valid.

```31:42:submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/structures/structure_bsp_definitions.c
boolean structure_bsp_port_verify(
	struct structure_bsp const *structure_bsp)
{
	long cluster_count = structure_bsp->clusters.count;

	return structure_bsp->collision_bsp.count > 0 &&
		cluster_count > 0 &&
		cluster_count <= MAXIMUM_CLUSTERS_PER_STRUCTURE &&
		structure_bsp->surfaces.count >= 0 &&
		structure_bsp->surfaces.count <= MAXIMUM_SURFACES_PER_STRUCTURE &&
		structure_bsp->cluster_data.size >= cluster_count * (long)BIT_VECTOR_SIZE_IN_BYTES(cluster_count) &&
		structure_bsp->sound_cluster_data.size >= cluster_count * (cluster_count - 1) / 2;
}
```

Proof is the host test command. This does not bind production arenas and does not run in PCSX2.

## Closure program C0–C7 (active execution plan)

The governing decisions remain in [plans/5.hcu-remake.md](5.hcu-remake.md). This section extends Steps 7–8; it does not weaken M2/M3/M4 exits or close unrelated M2 Steps 9–10.

### Product contract

- Qualify the 13 stock multiplayer maps and UI bootstrap map; campaign missions are excluded.
- Support one to four local players and Spartan bots in stock multiplayer. Qualify a minimum of four local players plus four Spartan bots; find any larger supported limits by measurement.
- Support cooperative stock team modes with human players on one team and Spartan bots on opposing teams, plus survival using Spartan bots, retail CE actors, and mixed rosters. Inventory and qualify every resolved retail combat variant in the retained source corpus.
- Survival waves are endless with a measured active-enemy limit. Dead players respawn between waves; eliminating all human players ends the match after that tick's damage/death processing. Bound wave, corpse, pickup, projectile, effect, and deferred-work storage and prove reuse.
- Preserve the pinned HCU `scenario_fog_at_point()` behavior. Record exact retail Xbox attenuation as unresolved; it is not a Step 7 closure prerequisite.
- Retain energy sword and gravity rifle. Use CE's generic fuel-rod weapon/projectile/damage path. Keep the accepted sword/gravity-rifle presentation adapters; fuel rod remains arms-only if its supplied tag has no first-person graph. Full weapon-family parity remains a separate gate.
- Apply the approved budget-v3 transfer of 262,144 bytes to `elf_static`; keep the remaining **5,505,024-byte reserve untouched** and preserve CE's 30 Hz simulation.
- Implement only the bounded M3–M5 prerequisites needed for these gates. Record those slices under their milestones; do not claim broader milestone exits. Network transport, SPU2 playback, and physical-console qualification remain separate gates.

### C0 — Freeze the baseline and acceptance ledger

1. Record parent, HCU, halo-ps2, Halo-64, toolchain, schema, budget, map, and generated-package identities. Hash dirty source files used by each build; commit IDs alone do not identify this worktree.
2. Maintain one machine-readable ledger in `docs/reports/`. Each row names the step/subcheck, member or behavior, owner, fixture and input hashes, evidence level, result, measurements, limitation, and acceptance requirement. Keep host, interpreter, and recompiler results separate. Partial, skipped, stale, failed, and missing evidence cannot pass a row.
3. Import existing consumer evidence with its original scope; do not repeat completed research or promote diagnostics to broader claims.
4. Generate the BSP member inventory from the linked compiler-derived schema and compiler-derived C layout. Cover nested fields, ordinary scalar and padding ranges, data blocks, references, counts, and pointer domains. Every byte gets a retained-byte check, documented normalization, or tested replacement. Require zero unclassified members and zero unexplained removals. Add synthetic structural tests for empty retail fields; retail emptiness never proves a field unnecessary.
5. Invalidate evidence when a dependency hash changes. Preserve unaffected independent rows; rebuild affected integration evidence against the final exact executable and package tuple.

**C0 status: complete (2026-10-23).** [The requirement-by-requirement closure audit](../docs/reports/m2-c0-closure-2026-10-23.json) passes all five requirements against [the regenerated canonical ledger](../docs/reports/m2-step7-step8-closure-ledger-2026-10-22.json). Validation passed **67 tests and 106 subtests**, with zero failures or skips; the audit pins the exact JUnit artifact and ledger digest. Steps 7 and 8 remain open.

| C0 requirement | Verified closure evidence |
|---|---|
| 1. Exact baseline | Parent, HCU, halo-ps2, Halo-64, toolchain, compiler/schema, budget, source-map and package identities are recorded. All 371 compiled translation-unit source hashes match current files. The package sidecar records byte-identical current reconstruction for 36/36 packages; 12 path-specific historical invocations are bound and 24 missing historical invocations remain explicitly unavailable. |
| 2. Complete acceptance accounting | All 75 rows contain the required fields and explicit per-identity availability records. Exact paths recovered from existing same-row digests are now integrated into the canonical ledger. Interpreter and recompiler results have separate execution records. Missing paths, changed historical bytes, unresolved digests and absent workload measurements remain missing acceptance evidence; they never qualify a row. Both target settings files are unavailable, so the reported successful target queries retain a partial exact-run result. |
| 3. Preserve prior scope | All 11 swarm reports, 28 Step 8 reports and 15 MP consumer reports in 11 groups retain their original evidence levels, map/query scopes, hashes and limitations. Imported diagnostics remain partial. |
| 4. Exhaustive byte dispositions | [The compiler-derived inventory](../docs/reports/m2-step7-bsp-member-inventory-2026-10-22.json) has 164 rows across 48 definitions. [The final C0 disposition table](../docs/reports/m2-c0-byte-dispositions-2026-10-23.json) covers every definition byte with **zero unclassified byte/member dispositions and zero unexplained removals**: 98 schema-declared rows have explicit preservation/normalization policies, 54 schema-unrepresented ranges have compiler ownership and retention checks using the actual linked schemas, and 12 omitted root members have named preservation fixtures. All 41 block/data fields empty in any audited BSP have deterministic nonempty structural and malformed-truncation tests. The 82-BSP/24-map CBSP audit has zero round-trip failures. |
| 5. Dependency invalidation | The exact regenerated ledger has zero stale evidence identities and zero repository-source drift. Mutation tests mark changed package/report dependents stale while preserving independent rows. Current compiled sources match their build; historical executable/settings gaps remain unqualified and require new exact-build evidence in the later acceptance phase. |

C0 classifies **byte handling and evidence availability**. The raw schema inventory still labels 54 ranges as schema-unrepresented; their final byte disposition is retained opaque data, verified against the real compiler-derived definitions. Their runtime semantics remain open. The eight unused root ranges, marker dereference safety, target consumers, production rendering, bots, mixed survival, residency/timing and final executable/package qualification retain their C1–C7 gates. The ledger still has only 45/75 rows with complete verifiable fixture identities and 20/75 with fully path-bound input hashes; this is explicitly incomplete acceptance evidence, not a claim that future workloads have run. C0 is complete because those identities and absences are exhaustively accounted for and cannot pass their acceptance rows.

### C1 — Produce complete multiplayer asset profiles

1. Create deterministic manifests for UI, stock multiplayer with bots, and survival. Record source roots, group/path identities, dependencies, hashes, import decisions, package hashes, and per-profile memory needs.
2. Import every resolved retail combat actor variant from retained retail source data, including its biped, actor definition, weapons, animation, collision, mounted occupants, and secondary spawns. Do not execute campaign missions or import campaign requirements. Give noncombat/script-only data explicit dispositions and reject hollow actor references as spawn recipes.
3. Implement schema-driven tag import, reference remapping, and stable output identities. Deduplicate identical dependencies and record deterministic source choice for conflicting definitions. Do not synthesize stock-MP actor references.
4. Keep CE data resident for its actual pointer lifetime. Store render/media payloads in independently addressable chunks with integrity checks so a complete selectable roster need not be decoded into one permanent copy. Include only data with proven, live consumers.
5. Add the sword, gravity-rifle, and fuel-rod closures and separately sourced presentation assets. Resolve the two undecodable fuel-rod bitmap metadata records before admitting its visual profile. Preserve provenance for every custom source.
6. Reject missing, corrupt, or profile-incompatible dependencies before tearing down a loaded map. Keep sound identities and request metadata; SPU2 playback remains separate.

**Exit:** clean builds from identical inputs yield identical manifests/packages; all required roots resolve, every actor variant has a valid placement recipe, and profile validation fails closed on missing or incompatible assets.

**C1 acceptance (2026-10-23):** the six requirement results and exact artifact identities are maintained in [the C1 qualification report](../docs/reports/m2-c1-qualification-2026-10-23.json) and imported into the canonical ledger. C1 is complete only when all six results pass; partial, stale, failed, skipped, or missing evidence keeps it open. The acceptance corpus is exactly 27 profiles: UI, 13 stock-MP bot profiles, and 13 survival profiles, built in two independent clean output directories. The source roster has 336 actor-variant occurrences and 75 logical placement recipes; every survival profile must bind all 255 resolved combat/mounted occurrences. Compiler-derived sound owner-ID normalization reduces the current Beaver Creek survival diagnostic to 10,700 tags and 25,436,415 decoded bytes; earlier 62,433,862-byte diagnostics remain historical evidence. Each selected BSP brings its own schema-derived tag dependencies and a rebound CBSP chunk. Sword and gravity-rifle poses use independently checked HPOS chunks and exhaustive native sampler parity; fuel rod retains the governing arms-only policy. Both fuel-rod cube layouts have explicit metadata and optional-reflection dispositions, without a full visual-parity claim. Native CE validation and bitmap packet evidence must bind the final package identities. C1 host asset qualification does not admit a production map: EE fit, integrated rendering, live bot/mode workloads, target admission, SPU2 playback, and console qualification retain their later gates. User decision (2026-10-23): the C1.4 finding that native bitmap registration and queue-end are not yet bound to the running CE frame is carried over to C3 (C3.4/C3.6/C3.7), because the HCU image has no parent render loop; C1.4 passes on host lifecycle evidence.

### C2 — Establish bounded storage and production ownership

1. Treat the current 4,477,120-byte tag / 1,993,712-byte BSP / 4,014,928-byte render candidate as a starting diagnostic only. Recompute requirements from C1 outputs, including alignment, relocations, validator claims, indexes, renderer workspaces, and simultaneous staging.
2. Choose and version a production partition before binding fixed providers. Start inside the existing 10 MiB map/render backing and keep canaries outside every live span. Profile-specific partitions may vary at a quiescent map boundary; never rebind CE pointers after allocation.
3. Any repartition of committed regions requires a measured budget revision. Budget version 3 records the user-approved 262,144-byte transfer from reserve to `elf_static`: total committed EE memory is limited to 28,049,408 bytes, `elf_static` to 2,359,296 bytes, and the remaining 5,505,024-byte reserve is untouched. Charge ELF file-backed bytes to `elf_static` and NOBITS to `resident_gameplay`; count each byte, nested pool, staging copy, and alignment gap exactly once. The physical reserve is `[0x01a40000, 0x01f80000)`, excluding the separately charged high kernel/stack reservation.
4. Replace unbounded whole-section retention with bounded independently decodable chunks; decoded chunk size is at most 64 KiB, with larger logical resources segmented. Pin tag/BSP/source bytes until all CE and DMA/GS readers release them. Evict only resources with no live reader or gameplay pointer.
5. Audit every post-load allocation. Put recurrent path, bot, renderer, wave, and mode work in preallocated or scoped storage. Replace cumulative no-op-free allocation behavior for endless play. Preserve realloc copy/failure semantics; no steady-state SDK heap fallback.
6. Assign actual high-water, failure, and reset counters to game-state backing, nested object/actor pools, dynamic simulation, tags, BSP, render chunks/cache, streaming, scratch, DMA, and audio requests. Do not double-count nested owners.
7. Reserve all **1,179,648 bytes** of DMA storage for the renderer's two geometry chains and two upload chains. Remove the separate snapshot DMA payload and emit snapshot-derived geometry directly into the idle renderer chain. Account environment, initialization, and font packets under their real owners. The fixed ownership, bounds and idle-chain protocol remain C2 requirements; guest proof with actual geometry/upload chains in flight is carried to C3.10 by the user decision of 2026-10-24.
8. Drain renderer readers and DMA before releasing map/profile storage. For full replacement, validate the successor first, dispose CE while old tags are live, unpublish/release the old owner, then load the successor. Preflight failure preserves the old map; later failure leaves a safe empty state that can retry. CPU/cache lifetime ordering and failure/retry evidence remain C2 requirements. Proof of this ordering with the integrated renderer holding live DMA/GS readers is carried to C3.11 by the same user decision; an empty renderer state cannot pass that later proof.

**Exit:** selected production profiles have explicit measured fit records in named guarded owners; the selected-profile production loader boots and loads a multiplayer map in PCSX2; repeated CPU/cache use reuses storage; fixed DMA ownership and release gates are safe; and measured guest placements remain below the budget-v3 reserve. Record unmeasured profile/owner rows explicitly. The actual in-flight renderer proofs assigned to C3.10 and C3.11 remain open there and cannot close Steps 7 or 8 through C2.

**C2 status (2026-10-23, superseded by the 2026-10-24 verdict below): in progress.** [The current requirement matrix](../docs/reports/m2-c2-status-2026-10-23.json) supplies scoped evidence to the canonical Step 7/8 ledger. HCP2 now segments every logical resource into independently hashed payloads of at most 65,536 bytes while preserving its logical identity and whole-resource hash. Two independently migrated 27-profile corpora are byte-identical; all 27 pass both strict Python validation and the file-backed C preflight. The migrated UI also passes the native CE CTAG/CBSP mount, corruption/retry, and bitmap upload fixtures. These are host results; the migration preserves the C1 logical bytes and does not establish a fresh source rebuild or EE residency qualification.

The separate snapshot DMA slab has been removed. HCU binds the renderer's two geometry and two upload chains to the complete 1,179,648-byte DMA owner, and allocates exact snapshot and renderer initialization/font workspace spans with canaries in frame scratch. Snapshot matrix packets append directly to a caller-owned idle chain. Actual animated geometry submission and the combined drawing loop retain C3 gates. Same-map profile preflight now rechecks package, metadata, and retained bindings before authorizing CE teardown; corrupt inputs preserve the live owner, stale empty-state stages are discarded, and the host lifecycle fixture retains safe-empty retry evidence. Foreign-profile replacement and the combined CE/renderer drain still require integration.

The full survival archive remains too large to load as one CE image: Blood Gulch requires 25,612,462 bytes before BSP or rendering. [Setup-time roster compilation](../docs/reports/m2-c2-session-roster-residency-2026-10-23.md) retains the complete selectable catalog and compiles only the selected session's transitive CE closure before allocation, keeping that image resident for its actual pointer lifetime. Measured mixed closures can still exceed the entire map/render backing; no production fit follows from the payload measurements. The production partition, complete owner telemetry, post-load allocation/workload evidence, final guest placement, and C2 exit remain open. The budget-v2 numbers in this historical paragraph are superseded by the approved budget-v3 limits above.

The original C1 qualification is a retained baseline. Current dependency checks mark its affected producer, bitmap, and integration rows stale while preserving independent corpus rows; C2 verification uses distinct artifact directories. Rebuild affected integration evidence against the final package/executable tuple before closure. Status edits to this active plan do not silently replace the older plan digest retained in historical package provenance.

**Accepted C2 scope amendment (2026-10-24; supersedes earlier reserve/status numbers):** the user approved budget v3 and carried the in-flight DMA guest proof from C2.7 and integrated renderer-drain proof from C2.8 into C3.10/C3.11. The retained budget-v3 selected ELF fits its file-backed reservation; the earlier guest run that stopped at the legacy renderer-gate setup is superseded by the profile matrix below. The current fit table keeps absent measurements unqualified. See [budget-v3 evidence](../docs/reports/m2-c2-budget-v3-2026-10-24.json), [scoped fit table](../docs/reports/m2-c2-profile-guarded-fit-2026-10-24.json), and the canonical ledger. All older guest/budget observations below retain their original version and scope.

**C2 profile matrix and gap closure (2026-10-24; exit verdict: met as scoped, with the residuals listed below).** [The matrix report](../docs/reports/m2-c2-profile-matrix-2026-10-24.md), [guarded-fit ledger](../docs/reports/m2-c2-profile-guarded-fit-2026-10-24.md) and the gap reports ([bots](../docs/reports/m2-c2-gaps-bots-2026-10-24.md), [ui](../docs/reports/m2-c2-gaps-ui-2026-10-24.md), [reuse](../docs/reports/m2-c2-gaps-reuse-2026-10-24.md)) record one PCSX2 interpreter run per selected session package on its own linked ELF, plus the UI package and repeated-cycle runs. All 26 selected session rows and the `ui` row booted the production loader, registered a qualified partition, loaded their real CE scenario and BSP, and released with every owner/arena/cache failure counter at zero and boot end below the 0x1a40000 reserve. Minimum margins across the 26 session rows: tag 0 B (exact-fit span), BSP 2,608 B, game state 58,512 B, residency 863,816 B, reserve 394,918 B (full 28-row matrix rerun on current sources, 2026-10-24: all rows pass, only the reserve margin moved, down 128 to 384 B per row from the cycle-loop `main.c` growth; the earlier minimum was 395,302 B). `ui` (366 tags, 789,137 tag B, 2,048 BSP B, 92.0 s, reserve margin 447,654 B) passes from a copy of its HCP2 package whose manifest size field (`package_file_bytes` 1,980,506 to 1,985,546) was corrected; the source manifest is still stale. The extra diagnostic row `bots_sidewinder` failed earlier because the migrated v2 bots package kept 19 sound-permutation data extents sourced from `c40` and `a50`, outside the 48,048,128-byte Sidewinder map; the package was rebuilt with the current producer (19 extents made metadata-only) and the row now passes (251.1 s, tag 4,584,878/4,584,880). The package was later regenerated through its producer with no pin bypass (HCP byte-identical) and the row re-ran in the rerun (241.0 s, reserve margin 424,998 B); the row is a Spartan-only lower bound and is not one of the 26 selected rows.

**Repeated use.** The production guest can loop load, map-loaded, release `N` times (`tools/c2_profile_boot_matrix.py --cycles N [--ticks T]`). All 26 selected rows plus `ui` passed at least 2 cycles (no ticks on most rows); `longest_mounted_gunner_4p4bot` passed 3 cycles with and without 300 ticks per cycle, `bloodgulch_mounted_gunner_4p4bot` 3 cycles with 300 ticks, `ratrace_marine_shotgun_4p4bot` (0-byte tag margin) 3 cycles without ticks, and `sidewinder_marine_shotgun_4p4bot` 2 cycles with 300 ticks. In every run, owner used/high/failures, tag and BSP cache current/peak, and backing used/high are identical across cycles 2..N with zero failures, owner and cache resets rise by exactly 1 per cycle, and the `streaming_map_owner` arena ends at its single-cycle value. Two 3-cycle sidewinder attempts stopped at the 600 s guest bound after cycle 2 (about 177 s per sidewinder load under the interpreter) and are recorded as failures. The ticks are one local player with no bots and no renderer, not the 4-player/4-bot workload; the tick-enabled ELFs are larger and still below the reserve (221,742 B to 232,622 B margin).

**Per-requirement results under the 2026-10-24 amendments (budget v3; C2.7 and C2.8 guest proofs carried to C3.10 and C3.11):**
- C2.1 partial: 26 selected packages plus UI have measured guest fits at load/release; a source-clean C1 requalification, the simultaneous renderer/index/staging live set and spawned-bot workload peaks are not established.
- C2.2 partial: a versioned partition registered `qualified=1` with canaries in all 27 rows and no live rebinding; `render_chunks` and `render_texture` stay unbound in the headless guest, so renderer-side fit is unmeasured.
- C2.3 passed: v3 limits, ELF/NOBITS accounting, 27 guest placements below the reserve (smallest margin 394,918 B at default build in the 2026-10-24 rerun).
- C2.4 partial: HCP2 segmentation and 27 packages through the profile runtime pass; reader-lifetime retention against live gameplay/DMA/GS readers is not exercised.
- C2.5 partial: CPU/cache storage reuse across repeated load/release is now shown on all 26 rows plus ui; steady-state path, bot, wave and mode allocation traces and endless-survival reuse are not.
- C2.6 partial: ten bound owners report high-water, failure and reset counters in all 27 rows (and per cycle in the reuse runs); `render_chunks`, `render_texture` and `widget_pool` are unbound and unmeasured.
- C2.7 partial; C2-retained scope passed: all 26 session rows bind the dma owner at 1,179,648 B (geometry chain 32,768 qwords, upload chain 4,096 qwords) with no private snapshot DMA payload. In-flight chain proof stays open at C3.10.
- C2.8 partial; C2-retained scope passed for same-map reload: cycles 2..N run preflight, CE disposal with old tags live, unpublish/release and reload through the actual loader with identical storage; 11 host map-service checks and the native UI mount/corruption-retry fixtures cover the rest. Foreign-profile replacement, failed-preflight-preserves-old-map and failure-then-retry now also pass in PCSX2 for one pair (`boardingaction_mounted_gunner_4p4bot` to `damnation_mounted_gunner_4p4bot`: 32, 26 and 37 checks; plus `boardingaction` to `ui`: 32 checks), 30 ticks on the surviving map, zero failure counters, boot end below the reserve in every run ([foreign-guest report](../docs/reports/m2-c2-foreign-guest-2026-10-24.md)). That evidence uses the headless drain predicate only; the injected late failure is the service's post-unload package revalidation, a failure inside the mount load or the CE tag stream is host-fixture only, and other row pairs are unqualified. The renderer-drain proof stays open at C3.11.

**C2 exit verdict (2026-10-24): met as scoped.** The Exit clauses hold: every selected profile (26 of 26 plus `ui`) has a measured guarded-owner fit and boots, loads and releases in PCSX2; repeated use reuses storage (all 26 rows plus ui, 2 to 3 cycles); the fixed DMA ownership and release gates are satisfied for the C2-retained scope; and every measured guest placement is below the budget-v3 reserve. It does not close Steps 7 or 8 or the ledger rows for C2.1, C2.2, C2.4 to C2.8, which keep their partial results. Residuals: most rows ran only 2 cycles (cycles 2..N identity is then one sample), ticks ran only on three rows, and sidewinder_marine_shotgun is limited to 2 cycles; the 2026-10-24 matrix rerun linked all 28 ELFs after the cycle-loop edit to `main.c` and the successor-mode edits (uncommitted until the orchestrator commits, submodule first), while the repeated-use runs predate the rerun; no 4-player/4-bot gameplay peaks or renderer reader evidence exists, and foreign replacement and failure/retry guest evidence covers one pair plus `ui` under the headless drain predicate only; the `ui` manifest and bots package were regenerated through their producers with no pin bypass (HCPs byte-identical) and both rows re-ran in the rerun, and the tracked non-2D report stays at the version the 26 selected packages pin, so the rebuilt bots package pins a regenerated audit kept under `build/`; C3.10 (C2.7 in-flight DMA proof) and C3.11 (C2.8 renderer drain) remain open. Dependency pins in the C2 status were refreshed only where regenerated evidence consumed the current content (`tools/repin_hcu_c2_status.py`, per-requirement `repinned_dependencies`); `tools/repack_hcu_profiles_hcp2.py` (C2.4), the post-load allocation source inventory and `ps2_memory.c` (C2.5) stay stale in the ledger until regenerated.

### C3 — Integrate snapshots with actual rendering

1. Version the pointer-free snapshot to carry map generation, up to four player views/cameras/viewports/first-person nodes/HUD state, shared generation-safe object identities, transforms and node palettes, resource identities, attachments, and bounded effect/decal requests.
2. Keep simulation, animation, first-person, HUD, and effect decisions in HCU. The parent consumes immutable published data and never advances gameplay or reconstructs another animation simulation.
3. Fit snapshot banks and pose storage within frame scratch. Deduplicate shared objects across views, track bank readers explicitly, and never overwrite a bank or evict its resources while a reader is active. Required workload scenes must present all players, enemies, weapons, and vehicles.
4. Bind renderer chains, indexes, and workspaces from C2-owned fixed storage. Add one-frame begin/end operations: render one, two, three, or four views before a single submission/present; advance cache age and upload budgets once per frame, not once per viewport.
5. Use full screen for one player, horizontal halves for two, and quadrants for four. For three (user decision, 2026-10-25), player 1 takes the full-width top half and players 2 and 3 take the bottom-left and bottom-right quarters. Each view uses that CE player's camera, HUD, and first-person state. First-person weapon and hands draw at full detail in full- and half-screen views and at a lower packaged first-person level of detail in quarter-screen views (user decision, 2026-10-25: keeps four views inside the fixed `dma_commands` owner without a budget change).
6. Connect BSP visibility/materials, textures/lightmaps, sky, animated objects, vehicles, weapons, HUD, and representative effects through the existing PS2 renderer. Preserve source surface/material identities for parity checks.
7. Publish texture claims only after upload work queues successfully; hold source bytes through upload completion and fence cache reuse against actual in-flight chains and GS completion.
8. Add a bounded CE sound-request adapter and recorder/drainer. Preserve event, owner, position, priority, and playback parameters; do not report SPU2 playback as qualified.
9. Carried over from C1.4 (user decision, 2026-10-23): bind native bitmap registration and queue-end to the running CE frame. CE `render_frame` currently publishes only a pointer-free snapshot and the HCU image has no parent render loop, so this binding is delivered through C3.4, C3.6, and C3.7 rather than C1.
10. Carried over from C2.7 (user decision, 2026-10-24): on the combined HCU renderer, prove in PCSX2 that the two geometry and two upload chains use exactly their fixed 1,179,648-byte owner, snapshot-derived geometry is emitted only into an idle chain, packet sources remain pinned until real VIF/GS completion, and chain reuse/overflow handling is safe under one through four rendered views. Retain chain counters, source generations, fence observations and framebuffer captures. Static layout tests, matrix-only packets and standalone viewer output do not pass this proof.
11. Carried over from C2.8 (same decision): qualify map/profile replacement while actual renderer uploads/draws hold source references. Validate the successor first, drain renderer readers and DMA/GS, dispose CE while old tags remain live, unpublish/release the old owner, then load. Malformed successor preflight preserves the old map; late failure leaves a safe empty state that retries. Exercise both emulator modes on the exact executable/package tuple; a headless no-consumer predicate is insufficient for this renderer qualification.

**Exit:** the combined HCU executable shows textured/lightmapped maps and animated objects in one to four independent views. Require framebuffer captures and renderer counters; CPU snapshots or clear-screen markers do not pass.

**M3 C3 status (2026-10-24): open.** See the [11-row C3 status ledger](../docs/reports/m3-c3-status-2026-10-24.md) and [machine-readable evidence](../docs/reports/m3-c3-status-2026-10-24.json). The older Blood Gulch checker flags for C3.3 and C3.7 are rejected as qualification: mutable identity telemetry reported 146 unique bipeds, 106 unique vehicles, and 3,660 identity overflows; native bitmap batches were all dropped and per-owner source lifetime was not qualified. The same baseline failed C3.9 bitmap dispatch/release and C3.10 DMA emission, and showed no first-person draws, effects, or decals. Its ELF predates current source fixes. The GNU89 `isfinite` link failure was fixed in `b542851`; the current four-view retry has linked with zero undefined symbols and ELF size 2,183,940/2,490,368 bytes, selected the stock Slayer variant, and is still at map preflight, so no target result is recorded yet. The focused host suite passed 135 tests in 9.08 seconds, including native ILP32 and ASAN cases outside the sandbox; these are not guest qualification. C3, actual bots (C5), and Steps 7 and 8 remain open.

**Performance checkpoint (2026-10-25; C3 remains open):** the source-pinned Blood Gulch baseline/optimized comparison now covers one through four players/views, with exact held-frame RGB, canonical snapshot and native bitmap-request parity and zero recorded unsafe renderer counters. Measured render rates improve from 14.96/8.00/5.98/4.99 to 29.97/18.70/14.54/9.99 FPS, and optimized gameplay advances at about 30 ticks/s without warm-window drops. See the [controlled A/B report](../docs/reports/c3-multiview-fps-ab-2026-10-25.md). This is scoped performance evidence, not C3 exit: first-person geometry remains oversized, the texture pressure case still needs actual eviction/reuse coverage, and C3.11 needs a fully passing current-tuple replacement matrix in both emulator modes. The newer standard-capacity integration checks pass render/snapshot/bitmap/four-chain rows in both modes, but their build provenance must be reconciled with the current optimized source; the older rejected baseline above must not be read as current runtime counters.

### C4 — Close BSP consumer and failure rows

1. Complete the C0 member ledger across collision wrappers/materials/surfaces, PVS/portals/PAS, fog/weather/wind, sound environments, lightmaps, breakables, decals, detail objects, lens flares, markers, pathfinding, and runtime-normalized values.
2. Preserve existing independent retail-map oracles. Add coverage only for missing consumers and synthetic nonempty fixtures for empty fields.
3. Extend pathfinding to live units: surface selection, path construction/smoothing, obstacle avoidance, movement progress, recovery, and breakable-surface mutation.
4. Verify render mapping against CE surface, material, shader, lightmap, and cluster IDs. Presentation conversion cannot remove simulation-owned data.
5. Inject malformed counts/references/domains, truncated sections, overlaps, insufficient capacity, decode/CRC errors, partial relocation, upload failure, and late publication failure at each distinct boundary. Prove rollback or safe-empty state, owner/canary integrity, and successful retry.
6. Preserve pinned HCU `scenario_fog_at_point()` behavior. Exact Xbox attenuation remains outside the gate.

**Exit:** no member remains unclassified, and every consumer plus each new failure/publication boundary has passing parity and lifetime evidence.

### C5 — Implement bots and cooperative modes

1. Implement deterministic Spartan bots in the HCU fork as player-backed participants using CE player creation, actions, unit control, scoring, teams, death, and respawn. Keep bot slots separate from the four local controller/view slots.
2. Implement navigation, perception, aiming, weapon handling, combat, pickups, and stock-mode objectives. Use CE collision, pathfinding, weapon, and damage paths. Bot state and seeded random streams belong to simulation and replay hashes. Do not import N64 capacities, throttles, or cuts.
3. Use real CE placement, attachment, activation, update, combat, death, and cleanup for retail actors from C1 recipes. Synthetic actor allocation remains supporting evidence only.
4. Measure Spartan player-backed bot counts separately from retail actor records. Zero retail actors does not imply zero Spartan bots, and player-only tests do not imply bot coverage.
5. Qualify Slayer, CTF, Oddball, King, and Race team variants as applicable, preserving CE victory/respawn rules. Humans share a team against bots in cooperative team modes.
6. Add survival states `setup`, `active_wave`, `intermission`, and `game_over`. Default to CE Normal, fixed qualification seeds, one active-limit-sized batch per wave, and a five-second intermission. Cycle wave templates indefinitely without retaining completed-wave arrays. Respawn dead players between waves; no active-wave respawn. After all deaths resolve for a tick, zero living humans ends the match, even if the last enemy also died that tick.
7. Count mounted occupants, swarm members, and secondary spawns against the active-enemy limit. Reserve worst-case expansion before wave admission. Bound corpses, drops, projectiles, effects, and deferred requests separately without discarding live CE state.
8. Expose mode, roster, difficulty, seed, and qualified population at local setup. Reject unsupported profiles before map teardown.

**Exit:** real Spartan bots navigate, fight, score, die, and respawn; retail CE actors execute lifecycle and AI behavior; both cooperative modes work. Survival completes repeated mixed waves, respawns correctly, reuses storage, and ends on human-team elimination.

### C6 — Measure workload and supported counts

1. On each of the 13 maps, run one through four local players, four local plus four Spartan bots, stock cooperative modes, retail-only survival, mixed survival, and weapon/vehicle/combat/effect/death/respawn/restart/return-to-UI scenarios.
2. Exercise every imported retail combat variant using a valid recipe and its required tag/render/audio profile. Test representative hardest compositions on each map.
3. Measure per-owner current/peak/failure/reset, stack high-water, allocation reuse, texture pages/pins/evictions, upload and chain usage, decode overlap, frame/tick phases, GS completion, and dropped/deferred presentation.
4. Increase population one participant at a time above the required baseline. Stop at the first memory, timing, behavior, or presentation failure. Publish the highest fully qualified count per map/mode/profile; any shared menu limit is the minimum of rows it offers.
5. Run mandatory workloads for at least 18,000 ticks and survival reuse for at least 1,000 waves followed by restart and UI return. Require bounded owner peaks and no growth across equivalent waves.
6. Repeat deterministic input with zero, one, and multiple render passes between ticks. Compare normalized simulation hashes across schedules and PCSX2 modes. Preserve 30 Hz and the 33.3 ms tick budget. PCSX2 timing is regression evidence; physical EE timing remains a release gate.

**Exit:** four local players plus four Spartan bots pass all maps, both cooperative modes have measured limits, and prolonged mixed survival demonstrates stable storage, simulation, and presentation.

### C7 — Qualify exact build and close only passed gates

1. Freeze source, dirty-file, schema, package, and budget identities; rebuild the production closure and combined executable. Recheck affected source-backed symbol dispositions, allocations, imports, ELF placement, canaries, and reserve.
2. On the exact build, run map/profile load, render, workloads, UI return, reload, malformed-package refusal, and representative failure/retry in PCSX2 interpreter and recompiler.
3. Retain settings, logs, simulation hashes, framebuffer captures, owner telemetry, ELF/link-map hashes, package identities, and assertions. Timeout, skipped native execution, stale artifacts, or missing rows remain incomplete.
4. Generate runtime admission from passing map/profile/mode/population rows. Validate package identity and required capacity at admission; provider readiness alone cannot admit a profile.
5. Close Step 7 only after its member, consumer, rendering, and failure rows pass. Close Step 8 only after production ownership, mandatory workloads, residency, reuse, and timing rows pass. This does not close other M2 steps, broader M3–M5, full weapon parity, SPU2, or physical-console gates.

**Exit:** no required ledger row is missing, stale, partial, failed, skipped, or unclassified; admission reflects qualified profiles; the reserve remains untouched.

**Step 8 actor-pool evidence boundary (2026-10-22; source-pinned diagnostic):** [`tests/test_hcu_step8_ce_actor_boundary.py`](../tests/test_hcu_step8_ce_actor_boundary.py) now prevents the existing 4-player Sidewinder workload from being misreported as bot coverage: local `player_spawn()` creates player units through `object_new()`, while AI actors require `actor_create_for_unit()` → `actor_new()` → `datum_new(actor_data)`. The existing guest sample uses 3,107,656/3,162,112 B of game-state CPU backing (54,456 B free) and 92,768/1,048,576 B of its nested object pool, but creates zero actor records; the object-pool figure is nested and must not be added to game-state use. Reproduction passed for the boundary/source and workload-order checks: `python tests/test_hcu_step8_ce_actor_boundary.py` and `python tests/test_hcu_step8_mp_workload_source.py`. Report [`m2-step8-ce-actor-workload-boundary.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step8-ce-actor-workload-boundary.md), SHA-256 `9df2966b00a786aa85490cc479ed885b340cb3a68d031ce137253f9a69cae0b0`. This makes the remaining capacity gap concrete: source a valid MP actor spawn/variant path, measure its incremental unit/actor/AI/effect allocations and CPU cost, then prove 4P+bot/co-op workload high-water and clean teardown. Do not extrapolate actor cost from `MAXIMUM_ACTORS=256` or count the 4P player-only run as bot acceptance.

**Step 8 stock-MP vehicle-seat actor oracle (2026-10-22; host ILP32):** the 13 maps do not contain a resolved `actor_place` source. Raw Xbox tags, walked with compiler-derived CE offsets (`unit_seat` 284 B, `built_in_actor_reference` +248, `unit.seats` +740, `ai_encounters` +1068), show 0 `actr` tags, 0 encounters, 3 hollow `actv` stubs (`vehicles\warthog\warthog gunner`, `vehicles\ghost\ghost pilot`, `vehicles\scorpion\scorpion pilot` with actor/unit/weapon `index == NONE` and null names), and 0 wired vehicle-seat built-in actors. Sidewinder alone has a 1-slot actor palette and that slot is also `NONE`. [`tools/audit_mp_vehicle_seat_actors.py`](../tools/audit_mp_vehicle_seat_actors.py) relocates the three Sidewinder vehicle definitions and runs the real CE `unit_new` seat scan plus `ai_place_pending_mounted_weapons`; the seat scan never queues mounted weapons, a forced queue then still never enters `actor_place`, and `actor_data->actual_count` stays 0. `python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_hcu_step8_ce_vehicle_seat_actors.py` — **2 passed, 13 subtests**. Actor-array high-water stays the preallocated **489,384 B**. Step 7/8 stay open; arenas and the reserve are unchanged.

**Step 8 CE actor datum allocation/attachment/cleanup slice (2026-10-22; freestanding host ILP32):** fixed the source extractor to select CE definitions rather than earlier prototypes, then compiled and executed actual CE `data_initialize()`/datum operations and `actors_initialize()`, `actor_new()`, `actor_create_for_unit()`, `actor_attach_unit()`, `actor_verify_activation()`, `actor_delete()`, and `actors_dispose_from_old_map()` bodies in a bounded freestanding ILP32 fixture. A valid synthetic actor-variant/biped pair was attached, cross-links asserted, then deleted and invalidated. Report [`m2-step8-ce-actor-ilp32-attempt.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step8-ce-actor-ilp32-attempt.md), source SHA `54f4e20e0c88715884f76c8fae41e877d154b2cdda485f967e1c0c104d84b2ac`, report SHA `0a30865335d3c955c9f9bd58c59c958606c86212902d54da5e3669b55371f133`. Synthetic tags/biped and peripheral stubs mean this proves actor datum capacity only—not 256 spawned AI units, actor-owned objects/effects, update/perception/pathing load, four-player-plus-bot EE CPU/RAM high-water, or PCSX2 behavior. Halo-64's `n64_game.c:202-204` omits AI as an N64 cut; this keeps CE actor semantics rather than copying that cut. The broader Step 8 match-workload and production-capacity gates remain open.

Same-map reload must preflight while the original owner is mounted, invoke real CE `scenario_unload()` (which clears its published pointers before `ps2_cache_release_map_owner()`), then call `cache_files_give_time_to_precache()` so `map_loader()` performs deferred post-release validation and remount. That draft was discarded rather than retained as a failing pytest. Rebuild the fixture as a clean host-POSIX closure (modeled on `tests/hcu_scenario_bsp_switch_main.c`, without including `ctag_mount_main.c` or `_EE` headers), then require real UI tag data, CE publication/unpublication order, owner/descriptor balance, injected failure-safe state, and retry in one process. ## Remake M3 — Player, objects, movement, collision, and snapshot

**Milestone status: not done.** Groups E–G have returned report/test work; runtime exit still waits until M2 step 10 has a resident gameplay BSP. Group E’s source-order/input fixtures, Group F’s bounded snapshot storage, and Group G’s parent collision oracle are useful partial evidence, but no live CE movement/collision parity or render-schedule-invariance trace exists.

**Exit:** real player movement and collision use CE records and collision; test traces match with zero, one, or multiple render passes between ticks; the movement fixtures stay inside `dynamic_simulation` and `frame_scratch`.

[docs/investigations/m3_collision_owner_auditor.md](../docs/investigations/m3_collision_owner_auditor.md) already settles the owner: HCU `source/physics/`. **Step 13 allocator follow-up (2026-10-21; audit only, no capacity change):** [`m3-dma-allocator-next-slice-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m3-dma-allocator-next-slice-2026-10-21.md) independently reconciles the existing renderer chain plan with the current arena and finds the four configured PS2 geometry/upload chains already consume all 1,179,648 bytes of `dma_commands`; the HCU snapshot packet slab currently overlays that region without being submitted as a renderer chain. It also reports that `dma_commands` receives only 16-byte region alignment while BSP renderer allocation requests 64 bytes, and that `packet2_create()` uses `memalign()` which the combined linker does not currently wrap/account. Its bounded recommendation is to prove and implement 64-byte-aligned arena placement using only measured existing guard slack, route each chain buffer through a named fixed arena allocation, and retain all four chain capacities; snapshot consumption, VIF submission, and per-slot high-water/fence checks must be designed together before declaring the allocator solved. The report's focused suite passed 6 tests; a broader allocation-ownership test failed at the unrelated still-open M0 semantic gate. This is a blocker audit, not evidence that the allocator or rendered scene works.

**Step 13 independent scene-submission audit (2026-10-21; report-only):** [`m3-renderer-submission-audit-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m3-renderer-submission-audit-2026-10-21.md) and its plan alignment [`m3-renderer-plan-alignment-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m3-renderer-plan-alignment-2026-10-21.md) corroborate the central gap: HCU commits a camera/object/first-person snapshot and the parent has a real BSP renderer, but there is no production consumer joining them, no submitted VIF1 world chain, and no scene presentation. The existing blue clear only establishes video/GS presentation, and the black UI guest viewport remains expected because UI is not a gameplay scene. Halo-64 is cited only for retaining frame/camera/first-person/HUD state across a separate render/present boundary, not for N64 cuts or renderer reuse. These reports are architecture/evidence guidance, not a replacement for the code-level tests and exact guest screenshots specified below; Steps 13–14 and the M3 exit stay partial.

The fixture plus parent oracle passed **3 tests** under approved native ILP32 execution. #### Remake M2 — Fixed memory and runtime tag image

**Step 1 — Prepare the real UI package inputs**

- Extract `MAPS/UI.MAP;1` and `MAPS/UI.HP2;1` from the specified data ISO into the exact lowercase paths consumed by the `host:/` profile: `build/hcu-parent-link-probe/maps/ui.map` and `.../ui.hp2`. Verify exact sizes and SHA-256 before and after extraction; preserve the old five-byte `ui.map` only as a separately named negative-test fixture. The CE request starts at `D:\maps\ui.map` in [`src/stream/hcu_main.c`](../src/stream/hcu_main.c), and the root/path translation belongs to [`src/stream/hcu_file_bridge.c`](../src/stream/hcu_file_bridge.c).
- Validate source identity and file-length checks through the map-service preflight in [`src/stream/hcu_map_service.c`](../src/stream/hcu_map_service.c). The preflight must reject a missing partner file, truncated map, unexpected source hash, or path traversal before CTAG/CE globals are modified; an OS `stat` success is only input-presence evidence.
- Keep these retail-derived files as local build/test inputs: do not stage them, commit them, boot the data-only ISO, or infer that PCSX2 opened the files from their existence on the host. For diagnosis, regenerated `build/hcu-parent-link-probe/maps/ui-with-bsp.hp2` from the same map/schema closure with `tools/pack_retail_ctag_hp2.py --include-all-bsps`; the 286,720-byte result contains `CTAG`, `CRNG`, `HMAP`, `B000`, and `CBIX`, with one 2,048-byte UI BSP and package SHA-256 `a83ded5049128dc0287b975af9c17f4224e467ad5e10ffa079468934e6241e90`. This is offline packaging evidence only; the current startup path does not mount its BSP catalog.

**Step 2 — Qualify the real UI scenario load**

- **Historical failed candidate (superseded):** With host desktop access, ran `APPIMAGE_EXTRACT_AND_RUN=1 python3 tools/test_hcu_boot.py --mode interpreter --stage m2-ui --elf build/hcu-parent-link-probe/hcu-gameplay-machine.elf --timeout 180` and the equivalent `--mode recompiler`. Both used ELF SHA-256 `e3577ca6ffc8f6f25d98e7930db0741f6dccfe099ae6a8133710861f938d7df2` and source pins parent/HCU/halo-ps2 `70e2ed0cf2145a45d23aa07414a3a88c05155d4c` / `6933490b2d16bb1c637295b6a0f3880ba23a7d56` / `71183bb0a9adc586cd267b2780c82bbc12cfd95b`.

This is guest failure, not the prior Qt launch failure.
- Source evidence: the current UI source has one BSP extent at offset `0x800`, length `0x800`; the supplied UI HP2 has only CTAG/CRNG/HMAP, while CRNG's first resource interval begins at `0x1000`. `src/stream/hcu_map_service.c` currently mounts through `ctag_mount_hp2_verified_source_discover(..., NULL, ...)`, so no BSP scratch/catalog loader is registered. Connect the catalog and a generated CBSP section before rerunning. A desktop capture from the interpreter run is `build/hcu-ps2-closure/emulator-m1-interpreter-aac1d5229f8b4e6d8059163a279540bf/screen.png`; the guest viewport is black at this failed boundary, so there is no rendered UI evidence.
- Rebuild and rerun after the BSP catalog integration in interpreter and recompiler, retaining full `result.json`, guest log, settings hash, package hashes, and last marker. The required marker is `m2-ui-map-loaded`, emitted only after successful `scenario_load` in [`submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/game/game.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/game/game.c). A successful file probe, cache request, or BIOS return is not a pass; compare both traces through load and teardown and preserve any first mismatch.

**Step 3 — Recompute exact per-map capacity and working-set peaks**

- Rebuild the `--layer all` schema closure first and bind each generated audit to its closure ELF hash, source manifest hash, and tool revision. `tools/hcu_schema_manifest.py` defines the compiled schema input set; all generated reports must name that exact set rather than reuse historical `a4b…`/`b3f…` totals.
- For every supported map and BSP, calculate both steady-state and peak-overlap bytes for relocated tag structures, CTAG names/claims, `animation.data`, decompressed collision/BSP blocks, retained render data, staging buffers, and rollback copy. Report disk bytes separately from decompressed size and simultaneously resident bytes. Include the largest BSP (`c20_3`) and all maps exceeding either `current_bsp` or the combined resident/BSP budget; do not average away outliers.
- Reconcile the resulting maxima against the region capacities in [`config/memory-budget.json`](../config/memory-budget.json) and actual bindings in [`src/stream/hcu_memory_bind.c`](../src/stream/hcu_memory_bind.c). Explicitly account for aliases once, temporary old/new BSP overlap during a switch, and the unchanged 5,767,168-byte reserve.

**Step 4 — Decide external-data contract versus budget proposal**

- If retaining `animation.data` outside the resident tag image, list every direct consumer, not just the public animation accessor. In particular, compressed paths directly call `tag_data_get_pointer` in [`submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/models/model_animations.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/models/model_animations.c), while the schema declares `frame_info`, `default_data`, and `data` in `port/linux/game/tag_schema_models.c`.
- Specify a versioned typed data-reference contract: owner/source hash, offset and length, alignment, compression codec, CRC, maximum chunk size, cache/pin lifetime, read failure semantics, and the point at which data becomes visible to CE. Tests must cover overflow, out-of-range offsets, short reads, corrupt checksum, unsupported codec, unload while pinned, and repeated frame decode without heap growth.
- If direct CE consumers cannot all use a behavior-preserving bounded accessor, stop at a decision record. A budget proposal must name the exact region increase and corresponding decrease/other-store transfer, show every affected map’s peak, identify temporary load overlap, preserve all supported campaign maps, and leave the reserve unspent. No format or budget change is implied until this decision is reviewed.

- **Step 4 decision completed (2026-10-21; no format or budget change):** the source-backed animation consumer audit found that a faithful bounded external-data contract is not presently supportable: CE exposes direct `animation->data.address` consumers, compressed helpers directly call `tag_data_get_pointer`, interpolation may retain four frame pointers, and no stable per-record identity or target lease/tick-cost measurements exist ([`m2-animation-data-contract-feasibility.md`](../docs/reports/m2-animation-data-contract-feasibility.md)). Accordingly, the reviewed v1 decision is to preserve the current in-image `TAG_SCHEMA_DATA` contract and every memory-region size, leave the 5,767,168-byte reserve untouched, preserve all campaign-map targets, and defer externalization until typed identity/lifetime, parity, I/O, failure, and target high-water evidence exists ([`m2-budget-proposal-animation-data-v1.md`](../docs/reports/m2-budget-proposal-animation-data-v1.md)). The proposal records explicit M2 integration-owner acceptance (implementation-agent review, not user sign-off) against the pinned source/range evidence and current memory-budget hash; its decision-era animation source hashes and diagnostic-only source delta are also recorded. The independent gate audit and current-state corroboration confirm that the decision fallback is satisfied ([`m2-step4-animation-data-decision-audit-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step4-animation-data-decision-audit-2026-10-21.md), [`m2-step4-animation-data-decision-current-audit-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step4-animation-data-decision-current-audit-2026-10-21.md)). This closes only Step 4's required design/decision gate; it does not claim external-data implementation, map fit, Step 3 capacity closure, or overall M2 completion.

**Step 5 — Create and verify the all-map package catalog**

- Define a fixed-size or bounded catalog record containing canonical CE map name, normalized asset paths, source identity/hash, HP2 and HMAP extents, CTAG extent, BSP count, BSP indices, CBIX/CBSP extents, and expected section hashes. The current production UI-only gate is `map_leaf` in [`src/stream/hcu_map_service.c`](../src/stream/hcu_map_service.c); keep it unchanged until the integrator wave registers the catalog.
- Test every generated catalog entry against the package writer/reader, not duplicated expected constants. Include all 24 converted maps, each BSP index, canonical UI aliases, unknown names, duplicate canonical names, case-colliding paths, tampered HMAP/CBIX hashes, truncated records, offset+length overflow, and references beyond file end.
- Measure catalog storage and parsing scratch. The UI path already consumes 1,550,484 of 1,572,864 streaming bytes; catalog registration must not reserve additional streaming bytes without an updated high-water report. Catalog-host tests establish metadata correctness only, not runtime map loading.

**Step 6 — Verify CE BSP replacement and rollback transaction**

**Status: complete for the host transaction acceptance in this step; the capacity-qualified gameplay-map guest run remains a downstream Step 10 gate after Step 8.** The exact current fixture executes the real CE switch/load/unload body with the parent package owner, HCU PS2 cache/CTAG/CBSP adapter, halo-ps2 bounded HP2 section reader, and package-derived CTAG/CBIX/CBSP from both `a30` and `a50`. The `a50` candidate now also fails specifically at PS2 relocation/rebase with both HP2 and CBSP image CRCs valid; CE restores byte-identical B000, keeps the owner coherent, and retries in-process. The approved native ILP32 run passed **1 test, 2 package subtests** in 137.06 s. Executable/map hashes are `f1e20f7ddcfd5b6f6ba1779efa6ccc9ef8524554346a2f8d781f8f3d3cf7a156` / `c47315d6d0536b9d5b4ae6d33e09f85f32385901d09c1d325e50995b113009b3`; C/Python fixture hashes are `041620fd4ba1931af6187e2fe23fcdc6f0331965acae6ec84b5139379d3bce55` / `17852aa9821b4f615808034c8099f9b6c0a9d9d446a7867d74da4544d0313c90`. Package identities, source pins, command, and deliberately host-only limits are recorded in [`m2-step6-relocation-rollback-qualification-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step6-relocation-rollback-qualification-2026-10-21.md) and independently cross-reviewed in [`m2-step6-current-evidence-review-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step6-current-evidence-review-2026-10-21.md). Host arrays are overprovisioned and use POSIX reads: this does not admit a campaign map or prove EE placement. Step 8 must first establish a lossless map/live-set capacity design; Step 10 must then run real package-backed switching, rollback, safe-null, retry, and unload under PCSX2 interpreter and recompiler. The overall M2 exit remains open.

- Mirror the actual sequence in [`submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/scenario/scenario.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/scenario/scenario.c): request a different valid BSP; unload old BSP; materialize/relocate new data; run `structure_bsp_port_verify`; publish new `global_structure_bsp`, `global_collision_bsp`, `global_bsp3d`, and index. Inject failure after unload, materialization, relocation, and verification; require the old index to be reloaded and all globals to refer to the restored allocation.
- Assert before/after arena cursors, BSP pointer identity, CE global index, cache owner, file-handle count, and BSP-release count. Include same-index no-op, invalid index no-mutation, first-load failure with no old BSP, successful rollback, and rollback failure with an explicit safe null state.
- Use package-derived CBIX/CBSP fixtures and assert their source hashes and exact extents. The existing 82/82 catalog correspondence report is not a substitute for exercising the C loader and its rollback behavior.
- **Package-backed CE/PS2 transaction evidence added (2026-10-21; host ILP32 only):** `tests/test_hcu_scenario_bsp_switch.py -k retail` now compiles and runs a single fixture containing the actual CE `scenario_switch_structure_bsp()` and cache load/unload implementation, HCU PS2 `ps2_cache.c`/`ps2_ctag.c`/`ps2_cbsp.c`, parent `ctag_mount_hp2_with_bsp_catalog()`/catalog/resource-index/backend code, and halo-ps2 `hp2_open()`/`hp2_find()`/`hp2_load()` plus arena implementation. It opens the audited `a30.hp2` directly (SHA-256 `ce38764fbba687bb26d4f2eb8a6cf066e19e4b8b81521d6f70f23ce8d00f183d`), consumes its real HMAP/CTAG/CBIX/CBSP entries, and asserts both exact scenario-reference extents and successful B000→B001→B000 replacement. The test also checks rematerialized B000 bytes are identical after rollback; injects B001 owner-read failure, CE `structure_bsp_port_verify` rejection, and schema-boundary rejection and requires B000 restoration; then fails both candidate and old-BSP reads and checks CE globals/tag bases are null, menu fallback is entered, and parent scratch is released. The exact run passed with `PYTHONPATH=. HALO_HCU_M2_PACKAGE_ROOT=/tmp/hcu-m2-capacity-repro.P5qdmI/packages HALO_HCU_M2_PACKAGE_MANIFEST=docs/reports/retail-cbsp-catalog-package-audit-e4a97218.json python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_hcu_scenario_bsp_switch.py -k retail` (**1 passed**; ordinary sandbox run skips the native ILP32 execution because it raises SIGSYS, so this pass used the approved native-run route). It now includes the actual CE `tag_validate_tags()` and `tag_validate_structure_bsp()` implementation with the package-derived CTAG active, a caller-owned claims bitmap, and the seven compiled HCU schema translation units; only the validator diagnostic sink and deterministic failure injection are test-controlled. The same command passed again after that integration (**1 passed**). The map/package content and CE validation code are real, but the fixture deliberately uses 16 MiB owner and 16 MiB BSP-scratch host arenas, a host file reader for the package, and manually activates CE's bounded cache state. It therefore does **not** prove FileXio/OPL/optical reads, production-sized BSP/claims/tag capacity, real EE placement/high-water, gameplay-map admission, or PCSX2 guest behavior; Step 6 remains partial until those separate gates and the failure points listed above are closed.
- **PS2-only Xbox-buffer normalization found by the package test:** the real `a30` CBSP envelope carries Xbox renderer `vertex_buffer_count`/`vertex_buffers` and index equivalents as non-relocated serialized values. CE’s ordinary bounded BSP validation rejects those values because they do not designate arrays within the serialized BSP span. `port/ps2/src/ps2_cache.c` now normalizes those four Xbox-only fields to empty values after successful CBSP materialization and before CE validation; the PS2 renderer already consumes HP2/VU packs and the port’s vertex-buffer register/deregister hooks do not use these Xbox arrays. The fixture verifies zero counts and null pointers at the serialized-header boundary, while leaving CE’s normal range/schema/verifier path active. This preserves the PS2 rendering ownership split; it is not permission to bypass collision, schema, or `structure_bsp_port_verify` validation.

**Step 7 — Relocate schema pointers and preserve required BSP fields**

- **Multiplayer target-capacity query slice (2026-10-21; host ILP32):** the refreshed [`MP capacity fixture`](../tests/hcu_mp_capacity_main.c#L45) now runs Longest and Blood Gulch through the real CE scenario loader and collision/PVS queries with the exact candidate cache/claims capacities and the validated 1 MiB + 512 KiB streaming child arenas. It compares the complete PVS bit vectors and independent source-map collision hit tuples twice per map, tests active-map preservation on rejected admission, and unloads cleanly. The approved run passed **1 test**; exact current artifact hashes and source pins are in [`m2-multiplayer-production-capacity-host-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-multiplayer-production-capacity-host-2026-10-21.md). This supplies bounded-host evidence for two MP maps, not target execution. Later work adds package-backed wind/weather queries on all 13 maps and a Prisoner breakable hit/broken-miss/restore case (recorded below), alongside the older a30 non-MP comparator. Still open: remaining breakable groups/damage integration (only Prisoner is breakable in the stock 13-map corpus), exact-zero edge ties and any source-supported vertex/grazing case, portal/frustum/player visibility, bot fog parity, remaining location/geometry consumers, full listener/audio playback coverage, runtime CE-global/arena failure atomicity across loader stages, and target-sized query execution under an accepted capacity layout.

- **Multiplayer sound-environment selection follow-up (2026-10-21; host ILP32):** extended that same production-sized lifecycle fixture to derive fog-free sound-environment/background palette references from raw Longest and Blood Gulch BSP bytes, then invoke CE's actual `scenario_get_sound_environment()` with a controlled local player/camera and the real CE local-player lookup/tag getter. Both maps pass across two map reload cycles; each selector call checks palette/tag identity, selected background, two successive source-defined interpolation steps for all 12 environment values, unchanged BSP publication and collision-user depth. The raw oracle, source pins, package hashes, executable/map/header hashes and exact command are in [`m2-step7-mp-sound-environment-host-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-mp-sound-environment-host-2026-10-21.md). This closes only the fog-free listener-selector host subcheck for 2/13 MP maps; it is not sound playback/SPU2, priority arbitration, fog/water transition, EE/PCSX2 map load, or target capacity evidence. Remaining Step 7 gaps still include bot fog attenuation, all-map branch coverage, weather/wind, playback, broader collision/breakable cases, runtime failure atomicity, and Step 8-qualified target-sized queries.

- **All-13 multiplayer sound-palette raw-source inventory (2026-10-21; host ILP32 fixture):** the fixture now hashes and inventories BSP sound-environment/background references from every source MP map and checks the catalog includes all 13. This advances only the source inventory subcheck; no Step 7/8 completion is implied.

- **Latest integrated schema/validator evidence (2026-10-21):** the retail transaction fixture now links HCU `port/linux/game/tag_validate.c` under a test-local renamed entry point, compiles the seven CE schema tables (`tag_schema_groups.c`, collision, models, objects, render, effects, scenario), binds CE's claims bitmap, invokes `tag_validate_tags()` on the actual package CTAG before activating the scenario cache, and calls the real `tag_validate_structure_bsp()` at each candidate load/rollback. Target EE placement, PS2 cache-address relocation, and device I/O remain open. The schema walker additionally enforces four-byte alignment for in-image BSP roots, `tag_block` targets, and `tag_data` targets; the reproducible corpus summary in [`m2-step7-bsp-alignment-audit.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-bsp-alignment-audit.md) covers 24 maps/82 BSPs and records zero CBSP round-trip failures plus 14,799 aligned in-image data extents under schema ELF SHA-256 `72fe0034df7a70a13e26d95ef470006054e6e5ff5425170c89a80e98b2319ff2`. Direct C CBSP parser mutation tests now cover 21 rows, including duplicate/cross-domain fields, descending and unaligned relocation fields, row/image target disagreement, and out-of-image internal pointers; malformed parses leave the output view cleared. The nested schema tests now explicitly cover empty block/null-name canonicalization, one-past and overflow targets, data extent past image end, and caller-input immutability when the walk fails; they still do not model EE arena cursors or CE global publication.
