- Implement relocation by schema-declared pointer locations/domains, never by scanning every DWORD. BSP-relative block/data pointers must resolve wholly inside the assigned BSP allocation; `tag_reference.name` pointers must resolve to terminated names inside the materialized CTAG name pool. Reject overflow, unaligned or interior-invalid targets, invalid sentinel encodings, and pointers crossing the region end.
- Build small synthetic schema fixtures with nested `tag_block` and `tag_data`, empty and non-empty blocks, multiple levels of nesting, valid/null/sentinel references, boundary-valid pointers, one-byte-past-boundary pointers, and malicious overflow values. Assert a relocation failure leaves source bytes, arena cursor, cache publication, and CE globals unchanged.
- Run the production [`structure_bsp_port_verify`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/structures/structure_bsp_definitions.c) checks after relocation. Preserve collision BSP, clusters, surfaces, PVS, and sound-cluster bytes; classify fields by call-site evidence (lighting, surface, sound, collision consumers), not by field names. Tie each omitted/externalized field to a bounded replacement test.
- **Direct C parser boundary tests strengthened (2026-10-21; host sanitizer fixture):** `tests/hcu_reader_fault_matrix_main.c` now builds both one-row and two-domain CBSP envelopes and calls the production `ps2_cbsp_open()`. The two-row control has an internal BSP relocation followed by a CTAG-name relocation; resealed negative cases cover a duplicate/cross-domain slot, descending relocation order, unaligned slot, row/image target disagreement, and an internal target exactly at `image_size`. Every failed parse asserts the output view remains cleared. `tests/test_hcu_reader_fault_matrix.py` compiles this with ASan/UBSan and reports `hmap=11 crng=15 cbix=16 cbsp=21`; `tests/test_hcu_reader_fault_matrix.py tests/test_cbsp.py tests/test_hcu_scenario_bsp_switch.py` passed **11 tests, 3 skipped** (the package-dependent native ILP32 case was not enabled in this combined run; it was separately passed in the Step 6 record). This adds parser evidence but does not establish schema-walker correctness, actual EE destination writes, or CE gameplay consumers.
- **Current capacity blocker sharpened (2026-10-21):** [`m2-2026-10-21-capacity-candidate-audit.md`](../docs/reports/m2-2026-10-21-capacity-candidate-audit.md) audits all campaign candidates against current physical owner spans and concludes none fits: even `d40` plus its smallest referenced BSP requires 10,310,880 aligned bytes for final CTAG+BSP versus the entire 6,291,456-byte `current_bsp` region, a ≥4,019,424-byte deficit before other owners or transition peak. The CE claims bitmap is a separate 1,288,860-byte requirement at that hypothetical span and cannot cure the overflow. Therefore keep campaign admission closed and defer direct-to-final CTAG/CBSP integration until a versioned, behavior-preserving EE ownership/residency proposal reconciles all unprojected map bytes and simultaneous costs. The audit is a lower-bound rejection, not a measured guest peak, and does not permit truncation, reserve use, or splitting CE pointer space across owners.
- **CTAG materializer overlap guard (2026-10-21):** The public API already required a non-overlapping destination, but `ps2_ctag_materialize()` copied before enforcing that contract. It now rejects overlap with both the source image and relocation table before the first write; the range helper conservatively rejects address-wrapping ranges, and forged views with relocation-size multiplication overflow are refused ([`ps2_ctag.c:35-47`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_ctag.c#L35), [`ps2_ctag.c:161-179`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_ctag.c#L161)). `PS2_CTAG_ERR_OVERLAP` makes the refusal explicit in [`ps2_ctag.h:8-18`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/include/ps2_ctag.h#L8); `tests/ps2_ctag_main.c:49-62` confirms overlapping input is unchanged. Verification: `PYTHONPATH=. python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_ctag.py tests/test_cbsp.py tests/test_retail_ui_ctag_mount.py tests/test_ctag_mount.py` — **23 passed, 1 skipped, 6 subtests passed**; `git diff --check` passed. A fresh `python3 tools/hcu_ps2_closure.py --layer all --no-bootstrap-memory` compiled 369 units with zero failures; its rooted object is `dc11aee8b888b409313a4a2e559110d8b1d12be6ff24d2aafd7fd27167438c0f` and its source-disposition gate remains open with 322 rooted live externals. This closes only the whole-buffer materializer's explicit overlap-contract gap; it does not implement streaming or alter the map-capacity blocker.
- **Step 7 remains partial:** `tools/audit_retail_bsp_schema.py` invokes compiler-derived HCU schemas and `tools/ctag_schema_walk.py` to inventory BSP extents and construct schema-derived relocation lists; the all-map package path verifies 82 CBSP envelopes and source extents, and the integrated fixture runs CE's real tag and BSP schema validators on `a30` and structurally distinct `a50` CTAG/CBSP while exercising CE switch/rollback. The fresh retail consumer fixture now executes actual CE `collision_surface_polygon()` / [`collision_bsp_test_vector()`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/physics/collision_bsp.c#L1220) against the currently published, materialized collision BSP ([fixture](../tests/hcu_scenario_bsp_switch_main.c#L718), [runner links CE collision/BSP2D sources](../tests/test_hcu_scenario_bsp_switch.py#L171)), checks in-range surface/plane/material results, and maps returned leaves to CE structure clusters. It also checks each cluster's self-visible PVS bit and every encoded sound-pair accessor for relocated-span bounds, same-cluster zero, and symmetry. Exact source/package/executable identities and the **1 passed, 2 package subtests** native ILP32 result are recorded in [`m2-step7-retail-consumer-query-qualification-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-retail-consumer-query-qualification-2026-10-21.md). Still required: full wrapper plus independent known hit/miss/material/plane/leaf expectations; visible/hidden cluster and game-sound/environment queries with byte-preservation bounds; failure atomicity across CE globals/arena for runtime failures; and target query execution after Step 8 produces a capacity-qualified map. `tests/test_ctag_schema_walk.py` covers walker-level null/sentinel/one-past/overflow cases and caller-input immutability, but those do not establish CE global/arena atomicity. The a30/a50 fixture uses host-overprovisioned claims/tag/BSP/cache arrays (a50's actual largest BSP is 7,159,808 bytes versus the 6,291,456-byte whole `current_bsp` region before CTAG/claims); it is not production-fit or target evidence. Do not use envelope round-trip validity, host claims/cache addresses, or host validator execution to claim target relocation or gameplay behavior.
- **Step 7 PVS pair oracle follow-up (2026-10-21; closes only the named visible/hidden-pair subcheck):** The package-backed CE fixture now prints its first row-major non-self visible and hidden pair from each materialized `global_structure_bsp`; a separate Python oracle derives those expected pairs from the corresponding raw Xbox BSP extents in the hash-verified source maps, not from CE's relocated memory. Full source/package pins and interpretation caveats are in [`m2-step7-combined-pas-qualification-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-combined-pas-qualification-2026-10-21.md). Keep Step 7 and Step 8 partial/open; Halo-64 is only an architecture comparator and REA is inapplicable to this source/raw-map behavior test.
- **Step 7 high-bit fog-plane → region → fog-tag consumer (2026-10-21; native host ILP32, package-backed):** extended the exact CTAG/CBSP transaction fixture with a raw-Xbox-BSP oracle that bounds cluster, fog-plane, fog-region, fog-palette, and weather blocks and selects the first valid reference chain per BSP. In the audited two-map set that yields one selected fixture candidate, `a30`/BSP 0 (`a30_a`): cluster 0 reference `0x8001` → plane 1 → region 0 → fog-palette entry 0/fog datum `-304804769`; the fog region has weather `NONE`, so CE falls back to cluster weather index 1. The four `a50` BSPs have no valid chain. No claim is made that this counted every chain in `a30` BSP 0, and no synthetic candidate was substituted. The C fixture asserts CE record sizes/offsets, compares relocated source fields and plane coefficients, calls actual [`scenario_get_fog_region_index()`, `scenario_location_underwater()`, and `scenario_location_water_depth()`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/scenario/scenario.c#L619), and exercises null-position, negative-side, and opposite-side lookup plus weather fallback/water-depth formula; on the opposite side CE's location-water-depth API still returns its signed plane formula for an underwater fog tag (it does not clamp to zero). It verifies BSP publication and collision-user state remain unchanged ([`hcu_scenario_bsp_switch_main.c:1283-1388`](../tests/hcu_scenario_bsp_switch_main.c#L1283)). Approved native ILP32 retail run passed **1 test, 1 deselected, 2 package subtests in 135.27 s**; test, fixture, retained ELF, link map, and generated header SHA-256 are `14af200645ea1f935a29123d10b45c2b7741bb0c7ac47b755d749ef1d9c7060d` / `ea81d7507a148a22fff56c77fcedc944b8a2faa507773862add9879d8e16a20f` / `7f5efb8aec56e7694406b14f0cba134f67b22eadbc0bdda80667ec29489eebcb` / `fe1b96a4688c5a01dbadafd507867aa4a9da7d387d7a6a2560d67aab8d4f525f` / `ac05e8c5e874a07995a94c6c2629a3b89c450d73b478f032f61db9e5519eeee9`. Full parent/HCU/halo-ps2 pins, map/package hashes, candidate values, and limitations are in [`m2-step7-fog-plane-consumer-qualification-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-fog-plane-consumer-qualification-2026-10-21.md). This covers one selected source-backed fog/weather chain and its location/water-depth boundary queries only; it does not qualify fog arbitration, all chains, weather simulation/particles, BSP fog drawing, malformed-reference atomicity, all maps, audio, target sizing, or rendering. Keep Step 7 open and Step 8 capacity evidence as the prerequisite for target-sized BSP queries.
- **Cross-submodule documentation review (2026-10-21; prioritization input, not implementation proof):** the cited audit [`m2-step7-step8-submodule-documentation-audit-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-step8-submodule-documentation-audit-2026-10-21.md) confirms HCU's docs do not define a complete BSP consumer contract, and highlights the `halo-ps2` parser's explicitly unverified/omitted categories—cluster fog bit, unreferenced/shared surfaces, render nodes, decals, transition BSP, lens flare, predicted resources, portal flags, and mirrors ([`halo-ps2/docs/08-bsp-section.md`](../submodules/halo-ps2/docs/08-bsp-section.md#L26)). Treat those as required dispositions in the CE member/call-site ledger, not permission to strip. It further separates PAS/environment queries from sound event/resource decode/SPU2 playback, and recommends making each failure-injection checkpoint preserve prior publication/claims/cursors or a specified safe-empty state. The report identifies Halo-64 `port/n64/NOTES.md` as a guide to CPU state that survives a no-draw renderer, but its N64 ABI, 8 MiB budget, throttling, and AI/script cuts do not transfer. This documentation sweep sharpened the remaining Step 7 inventory/consumer order; code and raw-map evidence remain authoritative.

**Step 8 — Freeze bind order and backing lifetime**

**Candidate texture-owner real-loader follow-up (2026-10-22; host x86-64 only):** using the 4,014,936-byte texture remainder from the diagnostic repartition below, the new host fixture exercised halo-ps2's actual `world_load_section_with_scratch()` decoder and `tex_open()`/`lm_open()` readers on production-converter TEXS, every emitted LM section, and scenario-selected OTEX from each of the 13 stock multiplayer maps. All per-map section sets fit simultaneously in the candidate texture owner; Blood Gulch was largest at 2,144,672 B normal retained high-water (2,144,640 B raw + 32 B alignment). Injected CRC failures raised the worst transient owner peak to 2,550,720 B, then rolled back the owner cursor while preserving earlier decoded sections; 13/13 failure attempts returned HP2 CRC status, scratch returned to baseline, and canaries/alignment/range checks passed. Scratch high-water was 98,304/524,288 B. Exact evidence is [`mp-step8-texture-runtime-host-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/mp-step8-texture-runtime-host-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `5ee6cc3bf5ed854fc2fd8f4786caa25f4938f547587d80031b6bf7df92b463e5`, with source/pins and per-map figures; loader implementation is [`section.c:153-247`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/section.c#L153), production world callers are [`world.c:76-99,121-126`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/world.c#L76), parsers are [`tex.c:28-71,121-167`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/tex.c#L28), and runtime assertions are [`mp_texture_runtime_main.c:118-204`](../tests/mp_texture_runtime_main.c#L118). The test directly loads only TEXS/LM/OTEX sections; its OTEX wrapper is OTEX-only. It does **not** call full `world_load()`/`world_load_objects()`, retain BSP geometry or MODL/SCEN/ANIM, upload to GS, exercise eviction/render-frame overlap, or include simultaneous player/bot/co-op/effect/HUD assets. This is meaningful runtime-loader evidence that the baseline converted texture subset fits the candidate remainder on a host, not EE/PCSX2 texture safety, total map residency, or permission to alter production budgets/admission.

**Relocation-tail-inclusive candidate topology (2026-10-22; diagnostic host ILP32 only):** the first candidate failed on Sidewinder because B000 requires 1,984,512 B decoded CBSP image + 9,192 B relocation tail = 1,993,704 B. The revised test-only profile assigns that exact additional 9,192 B to the BSP slice and subtracts 9,192 B from the adjacent texture remainder, conserving the full 10,485,760 B combined map-plus-texture span. With tag 4,477,120 B, BSP including relocation tail 1,993,704 B, texture remainder 4,014,936 B, map owner 1 MiB, section scratch 512 KiB, and claims 808,856 B, the actual CE lifecycle fixture completed **26/26** load/query/unload cycles across all stock MP packages, including Sidewinder and Wizard. Adjacency/conservation and boundary/content canaries passed; owner high-water was 89,164 B and scratch 262,144 B. The successful-run report [`mp-step8-tail-inclusive-candidate-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/mp-step8-tail-inclusive-candidate-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `f8b8d25a8bc4c8b7accb95d73f09a7b886fca2eb996dcd370e5af4764e45a649`, and manifest [`mp-step8-tail-inclusive-candidate-2026-10-22.manifest.json`](../docs/reports/mp-step8-tail-inclusive-candidate-2026-10-22.manifest.json), SHA-256 `7953d9b4ba955b3eca49b9a792f851f3bb55c526f272223f4da3eb880d4c3d00`, bind the run: binary `09db4dfda456f3de333f6e3bfa6bc4caba8fc0919ca19d8af939b3fb5e82e5cd`, link map `6057cab9a4813bdebe4d39b467a0de0f1a9a446c413c923fcfe4c2486e51c764`, log `d4a26e123d89ed45ad84296c8fd9c8708163006086f176b67efb60ca4f8e3099`. The fixture protects but does not fill the reduced texture slice; it does not measure EE placement, texture upload/eviction, or live 1–4 player/bot/co-op asset residency. It also checks wind outputs for finiteness only; numeric wind parity is separately covered by the all-map source-derived fixture above. This is diagnostic topology feasibility, not an accepted production budget or map-admission change. Keep Step 8 and Step 7's target-sized BSP-query gate open until texture safety, all required runtime owners, and EE/PCSX2 high-water/placement are demonstrated; retain the production limits and reserve.

- **Status: partial; whole-map residency now has a quantified budget lower-bound failure.** The stable caller-owned boot backing remains the correct binding lifetime: [`hcu_main.c`](../src/stream/hcu_main.c#L178) binds parent/HCU spans before `physical_memory_allocate()`, [`hcu_memory_bind.c`](../src/stream/hcu_memory_bind.c#L19) places tag cache at the start of `current_bsp` and assigns its remaining tail to one active BSP, and [`ps2_memory.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_memory.c#L91) rejects binding once allocation is frozen. `tests/hcu_ps2_memory_main.c` exercises the historical provisional `4,477,120`-byte tag span plus `1,814,336`-byte BSP span (exactly the configured 6,291,456-byte `current_bsp`), checks contiguous non-overlapping post-allocation addresses, and rejects late rebind; `python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_hcu_ps2_closure.py -k binder_tracks_caller_owned_capacity` passed (**1 passed**). This is binder geometry only. The newer [`m2-2026-10-21-budget-repartition-review.md`](../docs/reports/m2-2026-10-21-budget-repartition-review.md) checks the largest complete per-mission pair (`d20` CTAG + `d20_hangar` BSP) and the exact current ELF/BSS, CE game state, claims, and kernel/system reservation: the lower bound is 30,087,556 bytes versus the entire 27,787,264-byte committed budget, already 2,300,292 bytes over before texture/lightmap, dynamic simulation, DMA, streaming, audio, scratch, rendering, or transition overlap. The best-case `d40` plus its smallest BSP is a sanity lower bound only, not all-campaign qualification. Thus no simple repartition of the currently committed 32 MiB budget is approved by current evidence; a lossless, pointer-safe residency strategy or measured reduction in simultaneous requirements is necessary, and neither may be assumed from compressed-size projections. Do not spend the 5.5 MiB reserve, drop missions, truncate fields, or split CE-contiguous pointer data across unrelated owners. Keep the 2 MiB combined-image cache bootstrap-only and campaign catalog entries closed. Stable bind-order and successful UI lifecycle evidence remain valid, but Step 8 does not exit until a lawful all-campaign topology, target placement/high-water, and repeated unload/map-switch evidence pass.
- **Updated capacity prioritization (2026-10-21; zero-cost-removal lower bound still fails):** the independent [`m2-combined-animation-bsp-capacity-bound-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-combined-animation-bsp-capacity-bound-2026-10-21.md) joined the matching 24-map schema artifacts and tested the most favorable arithmetic for all ten campaign maps: remove every candidate animation byte at zero EE/cache/metadata cost and every compressed BSP vertex byte at zero replacement-render/query cost. The aligned tag+BSP candidate still ranges from **9,168,640 B (c20)** to **11,525,120 B (a30)**, exceeding the entire 6,291,456-byte `current_bsp` by **2,877,184–5,233,664 B** before any other owner. Keeping other named regions at their existing caps, the same report's highly optimistic whole-budget sensitivity is at least **30,527,104 B** for c20—**2,739,840 B above** the 27,787,264-byte committed ceiling—and 33,178,144 B for a30. This is not proof that every streaming topology is impossible; it does disprove “animation lease + compressed-vertex subtraction alone fixes fit.” Therefore do not treat the animation lease prototype as the next capacity fix by itself. First produce a lossless owner topology and a measured, source-backed saving/reassignment of at least the remaining worst-case deficit while charging actual replacement HP2/GS/EE data, claims, metadata, decoder and transition peaks; retain animation/BSP experiments only as separately measured candidates. The XISO/schema/package/ELF identities and all caveats are recorded in the report; it changes no budget, map admission, reserve, or scope.
- **PS2 documentation capacity constraints (2026-10-21; no budget authorization):** the broad submodule documentation audit in [`m2-step7-step8-submodule-documentation-audit-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-step8-submodule-documentation-audit-2026-10-21.md) notes that halo-ps2 `02-specs.md`'s illustrative 32 MiB partition sums to zero headroom and explicitly still requires measurement ([`02-specs.md:111-130`](../submodules/halo-ps2/docs/02-specs.md#L111)); its converted `BSPS` sections are custom renderer packs, not CE's resident `structure_bsp` plus collision/claims, and therefore cannot be substituted for HCU's working-set measurements ([`08-bsp-section.md:201-217`](../submodules/halo-ps2/docs/08-bsp-section.md#L201)). The audit also calls for accounting separately for EE-readable tag/BSP data, EE staging, DMA payload lifetime, GS eDRAM, and IOP/SPU2 sample stores; PS2 architecture docs mark DVD/decompression throughput as planned rather than measured ([`01-ps2-architecture.md:145-167`](../submodules/halo-ps2/docs/01-ps2-architecture.md#L145)). These citations sharpen Step 8's owner ledger and measurement matrix but do not close the already demonstrated capacity deficit, permit budget changes, or admit maps.

- Choose one model and document it in the service API: reserve measured maximum tag/claims/BSP capacities before `physical_memory_allocate`, or use one stable preallocated backing with transactional subregions. `ps2_memory_region_bind` rejects late binding after allocation in [`submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_memory.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_memory.c); the entry allocation sequence is in [`src/stream/hcu_main.c`](../src/stream/hcu_main.c). Do not assume post-boot rebind is possible.
- The current 2 MiB tag-cache choice is a bootstrap diagnostic, not retail capacity. Blood Gulch's 4,477,116-byte compact tag image and Longest's 4,263,624-byte image were used in an earlier provisional arithmetic split of 4,477,120 tag bytes plus 1,814,336 BSP bytes inside `current_bsp`. That split is not a campaign capacity proposal: the newer all-campaign lower-bound audit disproves it as a complete product topology, and neither it nor any earlier estimated capacity may drive production binding. Retain the historic reports for context only ([`m2-streamed-ctag-destination-design.md`](../docs/reports/m2-streamed-ctag-destination-design.md), [`m2-next-capacity-design-review.md`](../docs/reports/m2-next-capacity-design-review.md)); the source-hash-bound current rejection is [`m2-2026-10-21-capacity-candidate-audit.md`](../docs/reports/m2-2026-10-21-capacity-candidate-audit.md).
- **Residency contract review (2026-10-21):** [`m2-2026-10-21-tag-residency-options.md`](../docs/reports/m2-2026-10-21-tag-residency-options.md) traces CE `tag_data`/`tag_block` helpers and scenario/BSP consumers to direct pointer dereferences. Therefore HP2 streaming can remove staging duplication but cannot shrink the complete stable CTAG/BSP images under the current CE ABI; GS, IOP, SPU2, optical media, and OPL paths cannot substitute for arbitrary EE-readable pointers. The only credible no-loss follow-ups are experimental, not accepted designs: (1) prove which BSP bytes are parent-render-only while keeping CE collision/gameplay fields canonical, then test any split against all CE consumers; (2) prototype a typed, pinned external-data lease for a selected field family such as animation, after auditing every direct consumer and measuring concurrent pins and I/O deadlines. Do not implement either as an assumed capacity optimization. The current evidence supports no lawful fixed repartition; retain the contiguous image contract unless an explicit CE API redesign and full parity/lifetime test replaces it.
- **Compressed-vertex candidate narrowed (2026-10-21):** detailed call tree, layout comparison, identity caveats and parity matrix are in [`m2-bsp-compressed-vertices-parity-audit.md`](../docs/reports/m2-bsp-compressed-vertices-parity-audit.md). Exact artifact [`retail-bsp-schema-a4-current.json`](../docs/reports/retail-bsp-schema-a4-current.json) binds schema ELF SHA-256 `a4b332b78c57bd5455721313aa8668b951fc93f589e56cd22bec23f014d1e973`, all 24 maps/82 BSPs, and 21 raw BSP capacity failures. `d20_hangar` is 8,517,632 raw bytes and has 3,311,136 bytes across 371 `structure_material.compressed_vertex_data` extents; the apparent 5,206,496-byte remainder fits a 6 MiB span only arithmetically. CE uses these bytes for frustum visibility and collision-hit→surface/material/barycentric recovery, while object/map lighting also reads diffuse/lightmap UVs, packed normals/tangent frames, and incident radiosity ([`structure_visibility.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/structures/structure_visibility.c#L551-L615), [`structures.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/structures/structures.c#L517-L615), [`object_lights.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/objects/object_lights.c#L591-L660)). The parent BSPS/VIF pack preserves render positions/UVs but quantizes and expands triangles, spatially reorders them, drops unclustered surfaces, and omits source surface IDs and CE normal/radiosity data ([`bspconv.py`](../submodules/halo-ps2/tools/halo2ps2/bspconv.py#L117-L165), [`bspconv.py`](../submodules/halo-ps2/tools/halo2ps2/bspconv.py#L232-L266), [`bspconv.py`](../submodules/halo-ps2/tools/halo2ps2/bspconv.py#L338-L350), [`docs/08-bsp-section.md`](../submodules/halo-ps2/docs/08-bsp-section.md#L40-L53)). It is not a drop-in replacement or proof of capacity savings. Any retained copy in an HP2 section or alternate owner must still be charged; any external lease needs the complete consumer/lifetime/parity redesign. Assign zero capacity credit until visibility, surface/material, and lighting query parity plus total simultaneous residency are measured.
- **D20 render-mapping probe implemented (2026-10-21):** [`tools/audit_bsp_render_mapping.py`](../tools/audit_bsp_render_mapping.py) reconstructs the converter's exact cluster/material grouping, spatial order, and 24-triangle batch partition, then checks decoded VIF corners against CE source surfaces. The frozen d20 map SHA is `30d8fc02bcd209a7ce03174d8cf6cf771dd3621837d9a2d02111718dcc5e792c`; BSP 3 (`levels\\d20\\d20_hangar`) source block SHA is `a8e8174997a793831bd0504484582172e3802688a51d6b32a61e30c5962001ab`; generated BSPS v2 SHA is `1442a7da4494a6be9ff45753e65a0fc72c8eb03e75ee94aeda2b80786c67c3bb`. All 49,859 CE surfaces map to one BSPS triangle (149,577 corner comparisons; no missing cluster routes); max Euclidean position error is 0.0018664 world units and lightmap UVs are exact. The audit also found six diffuse-UV components beyond the converter's ±64 sane range (max source magnitude 516), with max reconstructed diffuse-UV delta 512.0001; that is a specific mismatch needing CE-use/fallback analysis, not something waived by describing values as garbage. A candidate four-byte surface lookup is 199,436 bytes. However, BSPS itself is 3,876,512 bytes; against the 3,311,136-byte source vertex blob, `blob - BSPS - lookup` is **−764,812 bytes** if BSPS is an additional resident owner, before staging or remaining CPU-query fields. If already resident for PS2 drawing, it must still be charged once and only a consumer-proven replacement can be credited. The hash-bound experiment and limits are in [`m2-d20-hangar-render-mapping-audit.md`](../docs/reports/m2-d20-hangar-render-mapping-audit.md); [`tests/test_retail_bsp_render_mapping.py`](../tests/test_retail_bsp_render_mapping.py) plus the BSP audit suite passed **6 tests**. This is offline correspondence evidence only: no CE visibility/frustum decision parity, collision/light query parity, EE lifetime/capacity, converter format change, or map admission is established. Preserve the full source data and zero capacity credit.
- **Independent Group B capacity review (2026-10-21):** [`m2-group-b-capacity-review-2026-10-21-a.md`](../docs/reports/m2-group-b-capacity-review-2026-10-21-a.md) independently reconciles d20/d40 lower bounds, game-state nesting, CE claims, separate EE/GS/IOP/SPU2 stores, and the new BSPS mapping. No current repartition closes full campaign residency while preserving all maps, current CE raw-pointer semantics, named owners, and the untouched 5.5 MiB reserve. The suggested next avenue is a semantics-preserving CE consumer/API redesign with measured stable identities/leases—not a speculative arena resize. This is a capacity blocker to solve, not authorization to abandon M2 or reduce product scope.
- Define the unpublish order: stop CE consumers; clear scenario/tag/BSP globals and cached references; release BSP/CTAG ownership and file handles; invalidate render snapshots that contain pointers/handles; then rewind only transient map-owner arenas. Test that any attempt to late-bind a different cache span after allocation fails, then test map A → unload → map B using the same boot-fixed spans and verify no stale pointer survives. Do not rebind or relocate the final tag-cache address after CE has captured it.
- Add canaries and high-water assertions around each chosen map allocation. Exercise load A → unload A → load B → unload B and failed B rollback to A; check exact base/capacity, alignment, no overlaps, no dangling pointers, zero outstanding file handles, and unchanged heap counters at every boundary.

**Step 8 follow-up — viable capacity experiment (2026-10-21):** [`m2-capacity-viable-design-review-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-capacity-viable-design-review-2026-10-21.md) recomputes the ten campaign lower bounds from matching `2fba…` artifacts and finds no demonstrated lossless all-map fit. Its best-case `animation.data` externalization assumes every payload byte is removable with zero cache/metadata cost; even then the tightest mission leaves only **2,404,132 bytes** for every other EE owner and cache before active animation pins. This is not capacity credit. The next experiment is diagnostic live-set/pin instrumentation on every animation-data reader, followed—only if the measured residual can plausibly close—by a one-field typed lease parity prototype. CE's direct-pointer tag/BSP accessors remain a hard architectural constraint; this report does not authorize paging, budget resizing, reserve use, or scope reduction.

- **Independent Step 8 prioritization and conflict resolution (2026-10-21):** [`m2-step8-next-experiment-review-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step8-next-experiment-review-2026-10-21.md) independently recomputes the decisive lower bounds: d20 whole-image + fixed known owners is **30,087,556 B**, **2,300,292 B** over the 27,787,264-B committed ceiling; the optimistic animation-plus-compressed-vertex zero-cost sensitivity still leaves c20 at **30,527,104 B**, **2,739,840 B** over. It recommends the smallest safe implementation as an opt-in access/lifetime observer with no placement or behavior changes, and explicitly says its data would be locality evidence—not capacity savings. This resolves the older [`m2-capacity-next-slice-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-capacity-next-slice-2026-10-21.md) recommendation for an immediate Longest final-span implementation: keep Longest only as a possible separately approved diagnostic slice; Step 8's next work is owner/access instrumentation and a versioned whole-machine topology, with all campaign and map-admission gates closed. The D20 host lifecycle evidence below establishes real scenario load, sequential BSP traversal, and one rollback, but does not yet demonstrate actor/animation access, pointer lifetimes, or a D20 working/live set; do not substitute this lifecycle result for the still-required workload trace or measured capacity.
- **D20 real-CE host lifecycle and all-BSP traversal oracle (2026-10-21; host only, not PS2 acceptance):** [`m2-d20-host-oracle-load-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-d20-host-oracle-load-2026-10-21.md) records the regenerated schema/package and native ILP32 test through CE `scenario_load()`, the real `scenario_switch_structure_bsp()` for B001–B004, and `scenario_unload()`, composed with the production HCU map-service/HP2 path. It loaded 2,743 tags, injected a B001 visitor failure after destination bytes were written, and verified CE restored B000 byte-for-byte in-process; it then successfully decoded B000–B004 to 5,425,904 / 5,104,740 / 8,420,296 / 8,543,144 / 3,818,380 bytes one at a time. Each successful step asserts only its selected BSP tag base is published. A deliberately overprovisioned 16 MiB host BSP destination is needed for B003; owner high-water was 10,220,268 B and the 128 KiB decode-scratch span reached its full configured high-water. The test-only `HCU_M2_D20_HOST_ORACLE` profile allows only D20 for this fixture ([`hcu_map_service.c:305-317`](../src/stream/hcu_map_service.c#L305)); a separately compiled production-profile test rejects D20 before opening assets. Focused verification passed **1 test** outside the sandbox SIGSYS restriction. The converted CTAG/BSP strips Xbox render payloads and CE validator emits correction warnings for absent geometry references, so this is CE scenario/BSP load, switch, rollback, and successful unload evidence—not actor/AI/pose behavior, animation access/lifetime measurement, independent outer/inner CRC failure tests, PCSX2, EE fit, or a basis to open production map admission. The test also does not load all BSPs simultaneously; the 16 MiB host buffer is not a PS2 topology proposal.
- **Step 8 animation observer prototype (2026-10-21; opt-in R5900 compile pass, no runtime measurement yet):** [`m2-animation-trace-prototype-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-animation-trace-prototype-2026-10-21.md) records a bounded 4,096-event PS2 observer (`ps2_animation_trace.h` / `ps2_animation_trace.c`), diagnostic-gated CE hooks for indexed frame/frame-info (`model_animation_definitions.c:500-568`), x-offset bulk frame info and uncompressed default cursor consumption (`model_animations.c:387-450,855-1020`), compressed helper container spans (`model_animations.c:2160-2570`), and compressed schema-validation span (`tag_schema_models.c:862-950`). Both `python3 tools/hcu_ps2_closure.py --layer all --no-bootstrap-memory --m2-animation-trace` and the ordinary same-profile closure compiled all **341 selected units**; the diagnostic recorder object contributes **98,312 B `.bss`**, while it is fully inert without the trace define (`.text/.data/.bss/.rodata` all 0 B). The canonical closure was refreshed to **370 compiled / 0 failed** (341 CE plus 29 port units; 322 partial rooted externals). The focused observer and range tests passed (**5 passed**); combined manifest/closure/observer regression passed (**40 passed, 1 skipped, 370 subtests passed**). The trace-on/off host harness extracts and runs the exact CE frame accessor body and confirms event ranges without changing returned pointer offsets. This is not a linked runtime image, full-model decode/state-hash parity, a campaign scenario, or any capacity evidence. Container spans are candidate bounds, not exact compressed-byte dereferences; caller phase scopes, compact guest extraction, schema subrange events, and actual scenario availability remain open. Do not charge its diagnostic-only table to the production layout, enable map admission, or infer leases/residency from this recorder. The PS2 manifest includes the recorder source but it compiles inert when the trace define is absent.

- **Stable identity/context audit (report-only):** [`m2-animation-identity-audit-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-animation-identity-audit-2026-10-21.md) maps which datum/graph/animation/frame values are available at the object, unit, and first-person callers, which remain unavailable to low-level readers, and proposes (without authorizing) a fixed-width versioned context table with bounded exhaustion semantics. Use it to implement stable identity and parity tests; do not treat the proposed table or the current phase tags as capacity/lifetime evidence.
- **Animation-data consumer inventory and independent lease feasibility (2026-10-21; source review only):** [`m2-animation-live-set-access-inventory-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-animation-live-set-access-inventory-2026-10-21.md) adds the misses to an accessor-only plan: three compressed keyframe helpers call `tag_data_get_pointer()` directly; compressed schema validation dereferences `animation.data.address`; default pose bypasses the accessor through `animation_get_default_data()`; and melee `animation_get_x_offsets()` directly walks `frame_info`. It classifies movement/root-motion, object/unit/vehicle/device, first-person tick/render, aiming (four simultaneous frame pointers), camera/cinematic, and sky paths, and recommends measuring unique bytes separately from simultaneous pinned bytes. The independent [`m2-animation-lease-feasibility-independent-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-animation-lease-feasibility-independent-2026-10-21.md) recommends only a whole-record scoped lease as a possible prototype, with stable map/tag/graph/element identity, validator-before-publication support, explicit nested pin/release semantics, and unload invalidation. Its lower-bound review warns that even removing every animation payload with a zero-cost cache leaves 2,404,132 bytes for all other owners and animation pins, less than the 2,621,440-byte dynamic-simulation capacity by itself; no capacity savings are approved. `tests/test_animation_data_ranges.py` was strengthened to assert the compressed-data pointers, schema-validator direct pointer, default-data macro, and both `frame_info` access forms; it passes **2 tests**. This static inventory is preparation only: no live-set/pin instrumentation or representative gameplay workload can be claimed, and Halo-64's N64 frame LRU is a seam comparison—not a PS2 policy. Preserve in-image CE semantics until the full instrumentation, parity, I/O timing, and physical memory evidence exists.
- **Combined best-case animation+BSP lower bound (2026-10-21; arithmetic only):** the independent [`m2-combined-animation-bsp-capacity-bound-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-combined-animation-bsp-capacity-bound-2026-10-21.md) joins the working-set, animation-range, and BSP-schema reports by exact schema ELF hash and each retail map's source SHA. Even granting zero EE residency to all removed animation bytes, zero cost to all BSP replacement render data, and zero adapter/staging cost, each campaign's aligned candidate tag+BSP pair is **9,168,640–11,525,120 bytes** versus the current 6,291,456-byte `current_bsp` span. Its broader sensitivity calculation retaining other named owner capacities is still over the committed EE budget for the best c20 case; this does not prove every possible measured repartition impossible, but it rules out these two uncharged arithmetic subtractions as a solution. Durable regression [`test_m2_combined_capacity_bound.py`](../tests/test_m2_combined_capacity_bound.py) checks the matching schema hash, per-map source-hash joins, full ten-map recomputation, and all ten exact aligned pair totals; **1 passed**. Positive savings remain zero until replacement bytes are placed/charged and CE collision, visibility, surface/material, UV, normal/radiosity and animation parity plus real high-water are proven. Halo-64's accessor-level N64 stream remains comparison-only and gives no PS2 memory credit.
- **Independent campaign topology review and ordering correction (2026-10-21; source-backed lower bound):** [`m2-campaign-capacity-topology-review-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-campaign-capacity-topology-review-2026-10-21.md) confirms that the d20 complete tag+BSP pair plus only fixed owners with known sizes already totals **30,087,556 B**, **2,300,292 B over** the committed 27,787,264 B ceiling before textures, dynamic simulation, DMA, audio, frame scratch, or transitions. This rules out treating a repartition or HP2 codec streaming by itself as the capacity solution; no arena sizes or reserve use are approved. It also corrects experiment priority: measure a complete d20 owner/live-set and all direct animation-data consumers first; prototype a whole-record animation lease only if measured residual could plausibly close, because even the optimistic removal of every animation payload leaves only 2,751,784 B for all other omitted owners. Longest is useful for a multiplayer loader vertical slice, not campaign capacity. Keep catalog admission UI-only until a lossless all-campaign owner topology, target placement, and real peak evidence exist.
- **Bounded animation identity sidecar and temporal context implemented (2026-10-21; opt-in compile/link, PCSX2 blocked before guest start):** [`m2-animation-context-implementation-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-animation-context-implementation-2026-10-21.md) records the replacement for the identity audit's report-only proposal. `ps2_animation_trace_event` remains 24 bytes on R5900; its former phase slot is now a one-based `context_token` indexing a 28-byte row. Pair binding rejects mismatch, overlap, misalignment, wrap, oversize, and duplicate binds; event overflow invalidates the capture. The 4,096-row sidecar costs **114,688 B** beyond the 98,304-B event array, allocated from `resident_gameplay` only in the diagnostic profile ([`hcu_main.c`](../src/stream/hcu_main.c#L286-L317)). Object primary reads carry datum+graph+animation+frame. Unit action/overlay/seat/emotion/aiming branches carry their selected animation and exact discrete frame; aiming/looking/continuous branches explicitly mark frame unknown. First person carries owner+weapon+graph, exact state/current-next frame, plus firing/moving/jitter animation identities; directional camera overlays carry overlay-animation identity with frame unknown. Map generation, monotonic `game_tick()` counter, and PS2 CE render frame are now copied from parent map loader/unloader, `game_tick()`, and `ps2_render_frame_state_advance()` respectively. Compressed container spans remain conservative access bounds—not exact byte dereferences or leases. At this entry's snapshot the focused suite was **58 passed, 1 skipped, 370 subtests** and the closure hash below was current; the following Step 8 export/decoder entry supersedes its test count and reports the rebuilt machine ELF. The PCSX2 result [`emulator-m1-interpreter-f80b0739dad14bb5ac903971c446a6e1/result.json`](../build/hcu-ps2-closure/emulator-m1-interpreter-f80b0739dad14bb5ac903971c446a6e1/result.json), SHA-256 `7b24febdc2f446895a2197a0ec5393d2ba5d7964fef6cee767fc2851a26df420`, binds that earlier ELF but exits before guest start because Qt/XCB cannot open X11 (Xvfb retry also failed). At that point there was no guest marker, screenshot, physical-placement observation, representative pose sample, guest export invocation, trace-on/off CE pose/RNG parity, or source-pin-checked decoder. The host stream format/decoder contract is described in the immediately following implementation entry; these still require a supported guest extraction path and a real capture. Keep map admission and leases closed; Step 8 remains partial.

- **Step 8 export/decoder implementation slice (2026-10-21; host contract tests and R5900 compile/link only):** added the versioned `H2ANIMT1` sink API in [`ps2_animation_trace.h`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/include/ps2_animation_trace.h) and [`ps2_animation_trace.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_animation_trace.c). It writes a fixed 28-byte little-endian header plus one 24-byte event/28-byte context pair per event, computes payload CRC-32 without allocating a second capture buffer, normalizes zero-token rows, and refuses incomplete/malformed trace state. The offline tool [`decode_ps2_animation_trace.py`](../tools/decode_ps2_animation_trace.py) enforces a 32,767-row maximum, exact header/length/CRC, site/admission/range/token/context-mask consistency, required provenance fields, and a recomputed exact-ELF SHA-256. The actual C exporter is piped through a host test sink into the decoder; negative cases cover dropped captures, truncation, CRC corruption, invalid paired-row token with recomputed CRC, unknown validity bits, and mismatched ELF pin. The current focused sandbox command including lifecycle and clock-owner fixtures passed **61 tests, 2 ILP32 sandbox-skips, 370 subtests**; the lifecycle's native ILP32 fixture separately passed **1 test** outside seccomp after asserting map-epoch changes (recorded immediately above). The refreshed R5900 opt-in closure [`result.json`](../build/hcu-ps2-closure/all-no-bootstrap-memory-m2-animation-trace/result.json), SHA-256 `6c1d0422e117d1a66fe4e36c46b13944cbe93ec00aaaa49c6245745445995b96`, compiled 341 selected sources / 370 units with zero failures and still has 322 open rooted externals. The recorder object is 2,844 B `.text`, but its exporter is dead-stripped from the current machine ELF because no guest sink caller exists. Rooting it is not currently admissible: exporter/encoder symbols alone are 568/396 B while the exact diagnostic machine ELF [`hcu-gameplay-machine-m2-animation-trace.elf`](../build/hcu-parent-link-probe/hcu-gameplay-machine-m2-animation-trace.elf) has only 252 B remaining under the 2 MiB static limit (SHA-256 `8222e9634d39a7ffa04c5d6d26ed261f7dbc34cd99cd58bb335f36d40763f85f`, zero undefined symbols, 2,096,900 LOAD bytes, 2,116,804 B `.bss`). The parent/viewer ELF remains over `elf_static`, so the combined probe is diagnostic only. A fresh exact-image PCSX2 interpreter attempt for `m2-ui` exited 127 before a guest marker ([result](../build/hcu-ps2-closure/emulator-m1-interpreter-aff9fa200c2647f68a52dc00dfc64eef/result.json), SHA-256 `46419cff5fd317b2470c73343df1ccc69b4b637252963e70bd98ff3b0d1fed27`); it produced no guest/export/runtime evidence. This closes the stream/decoder implementation contract, **not** a guest extraction path or representative capture: reclaim measured code space or choose a supported smaller extraction mechanism, connect it to an approved bounded guest transport, decode a real trace with source-manifest and dirty-worktree identities, and get nonzero context-tagged animation events from a real CE pose workload. Keep the trace opt-in, diagnostic-only; do not use it to admit maps or claim animation leases/capacity savings.

- **Step 8 real map-epoch lifecycle proof (2026-10-21; host ILP32 CE integration, not guest evidence):** extended [`test_hcu_ce_map_lifecycle.py`](../tests/test_hcu_ce_map_lifecycle.py) and [`hcu_ce_map_lifecycle_main.c`](../tests/hcu_ce_map_lifecycle_main.c) to compile the opt-in trace recorder beside the actual CE scenario load/unload and production parent `hcu_map_service.c`. The fixture samples the map epoch through the event/context rows after each boundary and now asserts: CE unload advances epoch 9→10; a failed deferred media preflight leaves it at 10; a successful remount advances 10→11; and the next CE unload advances 11→12. The native ILP32 run passed outside seccomp (`python3 -m pytest -q -rs tests/test_hcu_ce_map_lifecycle.py`: **1 passed**). Frozen evidence under `build/m2-ce-map-lifecycle-host/`: manifest SHA-256 `5464a25a6e572f3edd5f032c5c80d2cae40b8127fbbdbf553178a96b20f966c4`, ELF SHA-256 `e35c37f8646e5ec09a72496ec28d397cb605dde3856865330b04bd7286d8f131`, link map SHA-256 `43aee2acb0a6783d3f4018bee970f6396b8ab3e25d707d71ecf476e2ae1f016a`, and log SHA-256 `90fdafd56a4beb170e6f11f3b6df39d7954b2327a6e01f49d0c645125d64d56e`. This advances only map-generation identity evidence: tick/reset/pause and render-schedule independence, context-preserving whole pose calls, trace-on/off gameplay parity, target execution, and a representative nonzero pose capture remain open.
- **Step 8 deterministic tick/render clock-owner checks (2026-10-21; host C fixtures):** [`test_hcu_game_tick_fixture.py`](../tests/test_hcu_game_tick_fixture.py) now compiles/runs the extracted production CE `game_tick()` with the recorder and confirms one tick-clock advance after the active-game boundary, deterministic repeat after reset, and no extra tick for CE double-speed. [`test_hcu_render_frame_invariance.py`](../tests/test_hcu_render_frame_invariance.py) now compiles/runs both the production PS2 `render_frame()` and `ps2_render_frame_state_advance()` bodies; a 1-frame then 2-frame render schedule yields captured render indices 1 then 3 while the simulation-tick validity bit remains clear. `python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_hcu_game_tick_fixture.py tests/test_hcu_render_frame_invariance.py` passes **2 tests**. The map clock's real load/unload path is separately covered by the preceding CE lifecycle fixture. These checks establish clock ownership and decoupling under deterministic host execution, not alternate-schedule capture-hash equivalence, pause/resume behavior, target timing, or integrated gameplay tracing; those remain required.

**Step 9 — Implement L0–L6 as a transactional load with rollback**

- **Current streamed-CTAG production increment (2026-10-21; supersedes earlier notes saying CTAG had no production visitor):** wired a registered HP2 CTAG visitor through the parent owner into HCU's real `cache_file_read()` at the exact fixed tag-cache base. The parent now carries a HCU-independent `ctag_stream_info` transport type in [`ctag_mount.h`](../src/stream/ctag_mount.h), bridges it to HCU's PS2 API only in [`ctag_ps2_backend.c`](../src/stream/ctag_ps2_backend.c), retains the relocation rows in its map arena, and exposes a CTAG section-visitor registration parallel to the BSP visitor ([`ctag_mount.c`](../src/stream/ctag_mount.c), [`halo_ps2_cache.h`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/include/halo_ps2_cache.h), [`ps2_cache.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_cache.c#L645-L758)). The cache adapter copies into CE's unpublished tag destination, accepts only contiguous/exact extents, waits for HP2 outer CRC completion, validates the inner CTAG image CRC/header/table/relocation targets with `ps2_ctag_open_parts()`, rebases in place, and leaves CE's completion false on any failure; publication remains CE-owned at [`cache_files.c:1262-1314`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/cache/cache_files.c#L1262). Added explicit rooted HCU cross-owner entry points in [`hcu_ps2_closure.py`](../tools/hcu_ps2_closure.py) and assertions in [`test_hcu_ps2_closure.py`](../tests/test_hcu_ps2_closure.py), after the first production build demonstrated the internal `--gc-sections` root had discarded these parent-called APIs. Native ILP32 lifecycle now injects a late CTAG visitor failure after destination bytes land, confirms unpublished tags and cleanup, then retries; a fixture regression was also fixed by restoring its B000 selector after the preceding CTAG fault. Verification: CTAG/mount/UI tests **21 passed, 6 subtests**; composed CE lifecycle **1 passed** outside SIGSYS sandbox; HCU closure **370 units, 0 compile failures**; current exact combined ELF has zero undefined imports. PCSX2 interpreter and recompiler both passed the current exact ELF with `--require-physical-placement`, reached `m2-ui-map-loaded`, and remained stable 3 seconds with unchanged UI package/source hashes. Evidence, pins, ELF and log hashes, physical ranges, and dirty-source hashes are in [`m2-streamed-ctag-production-integration-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-streamed-ctag-production-integration-2026-10-21.md). This lowers post-mount owner use from 1,550,444 to 39,704 bytes, but transient preflight still peaks at 1,550,572 because it still fully stages CTAG. The machine ELF has only 2,044 bytes of file-backed margin under `elf_static`; parent-link probe acceptance remains false. Therefore Step 9 and M2 stay partial: implement bounded preflight validation without repeated random-read decompression, preserve CE publication order, qualify a gameplay map's complete live set, and do not infer visible rendering from this UI pass.

Both emulator modes now pass the same two-cycle UI lifecycle ELF, while failure injection/retry, streamed final-destination loading, and gameplay map acceptance remain open.

- **Status: partial (production final-destination CBSP visitor is UI-path-tested and now exercised inside CE's a50 BSP transaction with rollback/retry; streamed CTAG, full transaction fault coverage, campaign-map guest qualification, and L0–L6 closure remain open).** `ps2_ctag_materialize()` validates every relocation field, target, ordering/alignment/bounds condition, and absolute address before its first `memcpy()` into the final destination ([`ps2_ctag.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_ctag.c#L148-L207)); [`ps2_ctag.h`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/include/ps2_ctag.h#L31-L35) records the unchanged-destination failure contract. - **Final-destination CBSP stream is now wired through the production UI-map path (2026-10-21; host integration plus current-ELF guest UI marker):** HCU cache registers a dedicated section visitor ([`halo_ps2_cache.h:42-55`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/include/halo_ps2_cache.h#L42)); the PS2 cache recognizes CE's exact fixed-BSP destination/read request and streams header, image, and relocation rows into that destination, validates exact raw length and source extent, opens split CBSP parts, rebases in place against the mounted CTAG, and sets CE's completion flag only after all checks succeed ([`ps2_cache.c:500-590`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_cache.c#L500)). Relocation rows occupy the unused tail of the same fixed BSP span, avoiding a second arena lease. The parent mount constrains the visitor to exact CBIX catalog extents and rejects overlap with the tag image or indexed resource ranges ([`ctag_mount.c:108-139`](../src/stream/ctag_mount.c#L108)); the map service bridges HP2's zero-on-success callback convention and runs `world_visit_section_with_scratch()` from the bounded scratch arena ([`hcu_map_service.c:88-120`](../src/stream/hcu_map_service.c#L88), [`section.c:73-118`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/section.c#L73)). After installation, the whole-section loader is unregistered so visitor failure cannot silently fall through to the older payload staging path ([`ctag_mount.c:332-349`](../src/stream/ctag_mount.c#L332)). The actual HCU cache→parent mount→HP2 visitor path has host integration coverage in [`test_retail_ui_ctag_mount.py`](../tests/test_retail_ui_ctag_mount.py) and synthetic fragment/late-failure/retry coverage in [`ps2_cache_cbsp_main.c`](../tests/ps2_cache_cbsp_main.c); the combined CBSP/mount/transaction/HP2 visitor suite passed **16 tests, 3 skipped** using the frozen package manifest. This supersedes the earlier statements in this step that said no production visitor existed or that the mount still used only whole-CBSP staging. It does **not** qualify a largest retail gameplay BSP: current test packages exercise the UI path and small B000 fixtures; the independent live-set report finds Longest CBSP itself fits the fixed BSP span, but CTAG and visitor scratch do not yet fit the demonstrated simultaneous production owners ([`m2-longest-streamed-live-set-reconciliation-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-longest-streamed-live-set-reconciliation-2026-10-21.md)). No Longest/campaign admission, target EE high-water, CE collision/PVS/sound consumer parity, or in-game rendering follows from these tests.

- **Late outer-HP2-CRC failure through the composed retail UI CBSP callback (2026-10-21; host integration):** `tests/ctag_mount_main.c` now flips the B000 directory CRC byte only when the production HP2 reader opens the package header, after mount/preflight and after the destination is filled with a known sentinel. The real `world_visit_section_with_scratch()` / `hp2_load_stream()` path decodes and writes candidate bytes into the exact fixed BSP destination, then detects the bad outer section CRC. The HCU cache adapter leaves CE's completion flag false; the mount/source remain usable, no BSP lease is published, and the scratch cursor returns to zero. The test proves the destination changed (the late-failure case explicitly allows an unpublished candidate prefix), disables the one-shot fault, retries the identical read in the same process, and compares the entire resulting destination against the pre-fault successful output byte-for-byte. This complements—not replaces—the earlier transport EOF test, which fails before sink output and therefore requires the destination to remain unchanged. The fixture identities are [`ctag_mount_main.c`](../tests/ctag_mount_main.c) SHA-256 `8166f3d8db33f78cc4d864de20185565f2e1f2f78fc39d7d5c600dcb7ec53ddb` and [`test_retail_ui_ctag_mount.py`](../tests/test_retail_ui_ctag_mount.py) SHA-256 `5f47a23cfcd4032905fd1be04aeb7785f81f0c0dfe1e4ff8d5cc3b151a918d7e`. Scope remains the small UI B000 fixture and host FileXio shim; no CE scenario-global switch fault, campaign BSP, target device, or gameplay behavior is implied.

- **Capacity-design review (2026-10-21):** the report [`m2-2026-10-21-budget-repartition-review.md`](../docs/reports/m2-2026-10-21-budget-repartition-review.md#L46) gives a 30,087,556-byte d20 complete-CTAG + largest-BSP lower bound including exact fixed owners, already 2,300,292 bytes above the 27,787,264-byte committed budget before other live runtime owners. The independent review confirmed that CE `tag_block`/`tag_data` direct-address consumers ([`tag_groups.h`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/tag_files/tag_groups.h#L87-L145), [`tag_groups.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/tag_files/tag_groups.c#L91-L114)) prohibit a transparent file-backed pointer substitute. Do not respond by simply enlarging `current_bsp`, using external hardware stores as CPU memory, or enabling maps from compressed-size projections. The evidence-supported next capacity experiment is a per-field ownership/consumer differential for large BSP `compressed_vertex_data` (3,311,136 bytes in d20_hangar) and typed animation leases (4,490,730 bytes in d20): CE visibility directly dereferences BSP compressed vertices ([`structure_visibility.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/structures/structure_visibility.c#L595-L601)), while animation has multi-pointer consumers and no established safe lease contract. Both are candidates only; quantify all remaining owners and prove byte/behavior parity before counting savings.

- Map each loader stage to a concrete function and observable state: L0 catalog/path/length; L1 HP2 table/codec/extent/hash validation; L2 bounded region reservation and CTAG/claims validation; L3 cache span binding and `cache_file_tag_header_verify`; L4 schema validation then tag publication; L5 selected BSP relocation and `structure_bsp_port_verify`; L6 required presentation sections plus single commit. Relevant owners are [`src/stream/hcu_map_service.c`](../src/stream/hcu_map_service.c), [`submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_cache.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_cache.c), and [`submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_cbsp.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_cbsp.c).

Exact ordinary-profile UI load passed in both PCSX2 modes and the diagnostic profile passed two same-process reload cycles in both modes. Still add failures at tag read, tag-header/schema validation, BSP setup, and post-unload media read; prove centralized abort clears CE globals before releasing the parent owner and that a corrected source can retry.

Teardown first clears CE scenario/BSP globals and unloads tag consumers, then releases the parent owner. The new lifecycle case in [`hcu_ce_map_lifecycle_main.c`](../tests/hcu_ce_map_lifecycle_main.c) wraps only [`world_visit_section_with_scratch()`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/section.c#L73-L137) for B000, forwards a decoded sink callback to CE's actual BSP destination, then returns failure from the same callback (so the 2,288-byte UI B000's possible single-chunk output still exercises a post-write abort); after the failed initial `scenario_load()` it asserts safe-null CE globals, unpublished tags, closed source, returned owner/scratch cursors, and corrected same-process retry. The runner links that wrapper against the exact production map-service callback ([`hcu_map_service.c`](../src/stream/hcu_map_service.c#L88-L120)) and records the intended injection in its manifest ([`test_hcu_ce_map_lifecycle.py`](../tests/test_hcu_ce_map_lifecycle.py)). `python3 -m pytest -q -rs tests/test_hcu_ce_map_lifecycle.py` compiled the full native ILP32 fixture, but runtime was skipped because this environment blocks native ILP32 with SIGSYS; the source regression checks are in [`test_hcu_ce_initial_bsp_rollback_source.py`](../tests/test_hcu_ce_initial_bsp_rollback_source.py). The final R5900 closure compiled 370 units with zero failures; the combined lifecycle diagnostic ELF `1e5927a09febbbdc15577e6188946b37706e6335b6e2d074c1b1cbde5f25621a` linked with zero undefined imports, but overall acceptance remains false. A fresh PCSX2 interpreter `m2-ui` run of that exact ELF did not start, including under Xvfb: the AppImage's xcb platform plugin could not connect to the display and still reported `xcb-cursor0`/`libxcb-cursor0` unavailable ([launcher log](../build/hcu-ps2-closure/emulator-m1-interpreter-adac580b9ac34d9d9dbc7792d17a4ac2/launcher.log)). Treat this as implemented-but-unverified behavior until the composed lifecycle executable actually runs and the current exact ELF gets a guest run. - Do not implement L2 by increasing the 1.5 MiB `streaming` arena to retain a complete CTAG copy. `world_load_section_with_scratch()` allocates complete raw and stored buffers ([`section.c`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/section.c#L141-L235)), and CTAG raw data includes a relocation table larger than the final image. Follow [`m2-streamed-ctag-destination-design.md`](../docs/reports/m2-streamed-ctag-destination-design.md): add a bounded HP2 decode visitor with a 64 KiB LZ4 history window and bounded input, stream-validate CTAG while preserving both CRC layers and every `ps2_ctag_open()` invariant, then after CE unpublishes the old map write/relocate directly into the boot-fixed tag-cache address. These buffer sizes are starting design parameters, not EE measurements; keep the existing whole-section API intact for small sections until byte-parity and adversarial tests pass. The current generic visitor is implemented at [`section.c::world_visit_section_reader_with_scratch()`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/section.c#L79-L138), but no HCU CTAG/CBSP production caller uses it; its sink may observe a prefix before late CRC failure, so the final span must remain explicitly unpublished and retry must rewrite from offset zero.
- **CTAG stream design audit (2026-10-21; implementer constraints):** [`m2-ctag-stream-audit-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-ctag-stream-audit-2026-10-21.md) confirms a full semantic/no-write CTAG preflight cannot currently be done within measured scratch: CTAG relocations follow the image, and validating every relocation target requires staging the image. Use the fixed tag-cache span only as unpublished staging, only after CE has unloaded/unpublished the prior map; keep the relocation array in separate bounded scratch (Longest: 75,420 bytes for 18,855 entries) and the visitor's 98,304-byte history/input/output workspace. Do not publish CE cache/global state until outer HP2 CRC, CTAG internal CRC, header/table/range checks, and complete relocation validation succeed. Since the visitor can write a prefix before outer CRC failure, keep the owner explicitly loading/unpublished, discard/rewrite from byte zero on retry, and test that no CE consumer can observe staged bytes. Existing `ps2_ctag_materialize()` intentionally rejects source/destination overlap, so preserve that guard and design a separate in-place staging/rebase operation with proof that relocation metadata remains outside overwritten bytes. Close source identity, retained-reader, late-failure/retry, and header-only resource-validation questions from the audit before production use; these constraints do not prove a retail map fits or the loader is EE-safe.
- **Split-span CTAG validation and in-place rebase primitives (2026-10-21; parser plus host composition):** [`ps2_ctag_open_parts()`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/include/ps2_ctag.h#L33-L41) validates the same envelope/image/relocation invariants when those three pieces live in separate caller-owned spans; contiguous `ps2_ctag_open()` delegates after exact-payload-length checks ([`ps2_ctag.c:93-205`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_ctag.c#L93-L205)). [`ps2_ctag_rebase_in_place()`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_ctag.c#L265-L308) accepts only the exact staged image pointer, requires the relocation span to be disjoint, and validates all fixups and base-address bounds before writing the first pointer. Independent review caught and fixed the legacy materializer's forged-view underflow when `image_size < 4` ([`ps2_ctag.c:208-216`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_ctag.c#L208-L216)); a regression proves it rejects the view without modifying destination ([`ps2_ctag_main.c:153-170`](../tests/ps2_ctag_main.c#L153-L170)). The unit fixture checks byte parity against `ps2_ctag_materialize()` and confirms a malformed final target leaves the staged image unchanged ([`ps2_ctag_main.c:69-100`](../tests/ps2_ctag_main.c#L69-L100)); the retail-derived Longest visitor composition and both CRC late-failure controls are recorded in the following bullet. The focused regression command across CTAG/CBSP/mount/lifecycle/schema tests passed (**45 passed, 2 skipped, 8 subtests passed**); the production HCU closure compiled **369 units, 0 failures**, and M0 remains open at **322 rooted externals**. Parser and host-composition evidence is not a production cache callback, source-identity binding, target-map/EE test, or changed memory admission.
- **Bounded Longest HP2→CTAG streaming composition (2026-10-21; host only):** [`hp2_ctag_stream_main.c`](../tests/hp2_ctag_stream_main.c) composes the existing `world_visit_section_with_scratch()` visitor with `ps2_ctag_open_parts()` and `ps2_ctag_rebase_in_place()`. It reserves Longest's known relocation span before the visitor lease, routes header/image/relocation bytes into separate spans, validates the full section before rebase, and compares the final bytes to the Python oracle. The retail-derived Longest package completed with **173,760-byte scratch high-water** (75,420 relocation bytes + 36 bytes alignment + 98,304 visitor workspace). Three failures are exercised: a sink abort after the image plus four relocation words are staged (`HP2_ERR_IO`), a bad outer HP2 CTAG CRC, and a bad inner CTAG image CRC under a rebuilt valid HP2 envelope. Each compares the entire staged image against the matching relative-image bytes, proving this harness does not rebase on those failure exits. [`test_ctag.py:235-325`](../tests/test_ctag.py#L235) and the broader focused suite passed **45 tests, 2 skipped, 8 subtests**. This remains host-only and does not prove CE completion flags/globals, production source-I/O rollback, EE scratch interference, package-source identity at the target seam, or PCSX2 Longest load. The actual production insertion point is the exact PS2 `cache_file_read()` call after CE poisons tag-cache memory and before CE verifies/publishes tag globals ([`cache_files.c:1262-1314`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/cache/cache_files.c#L1262), [`ps2_cache.c:375-415`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_cache.c#L375)); implement and fault-test that callback before calling host composition production integration.
- **Real CE tag-read late-failure/retry seam (2026-10-21; host ILP32):** the composed lifecycle fixture [`hcu_ce_map_lifecycle_main.c`](../tests/hcu_ce_map_lifecycle_main.c) now corrupts only the final relocation-table entry (an aligned field offset equal to `image_size`) in the mounted UI CTAG after parent preflight/mount, then invokes the real `scenario_tags_load("levels\\ui\\ui")` path. CE's exact `cache_file_read()` request reaches `ps2_ctag_materialize()`; it returns failure before copying, leaving the destination at CE's `0xCD` poison and keeping `tags_loaded`, tag header, global instance table/count unpublished. The parent mount and source reader remain live. Restoring the relocation permits immediate retry on the same mounted source; the fixture then unloads tag globals and proceeds through real scenario load/unload and the existing post-unload HP2 transport failure plus fresh same-process recovery. Focused native ILP32 command `PYTHONPATH=. HALO_HCU_M2_PACKAGE_ROOT=/tmp/hcu-m2-capacity-repro.P5qdmI/packages HALO_HCU_M2_PACKAGE_MANIFEST=docs/reports/retail-cbsp-catalog-package-audit-e4a97218.json python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_hcu_ce_map_lifecycle.py -x` passed **1 test**; exact fixture ELF SHA-256 `b0c78f83217de8bd02896cf11d63b24ecb6e2d1c3a956e0eb1f176bb2e463bfc` and report [`m2-ce-map-lifecycle-host-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-ce-map-lifecycle-host-2026-10-21.md). This is real CE/parent failure atomicity for the existing whole-buffer validator—not streamed partial-destination failure, not schema/BSP rollback, not target storage, and not M2 closure. The relevant publication boundary is [`cache_files.c:1262-1314`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/cache/cache_files.c#L1262), with the pre-copy relocation validation at [`ps2_ctag.c:208-263`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_ctag.c#L208). The independent review in [`m2-step9-ce-tag-read-corroboration-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step9-ce-tag-read-corroboration-2026-10-21.md) corroborates this exact boundary and explicitly limits it to the host ILP32 whole-buffer path.
- **CE read ordering correction from lifecycle audit:** [`scenario_tags_load()`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/cache/cache_files.c) clears the final tag destination with `0xCD` immediately before the exact `cache_file_read()` request. Therefore mount-time eager CTAG staging would be erased. A future streamed mode must run synchronously inside that exact cache-read callback after CE unload, and mark the completion flag successful only after visitor outer CRC, `ps2_ctag_open_parts()` validation, and all in-place fixups succeed. Keep preflight non-destructive while the old map is active; it may validate streamed section/envelope/image CRC but cannot claim full target validation without image staging.
- Treat CBSP as a separate whole-section capacity issue: `ctag_mount.c:67-104` currently stages the entire selected BSP in `frame_scratch`, while Blood Gulch B000 raw data is 1,303,092 bytes, larger than the 393,216-byte scratch region. Reuse a bounded HP2 visitor only after designing an independent CBSP stream validator/materializer that preserves source-pointer/domain checks and keeps only one verified BSP in the stable BSP destination. A CTAG stream change alone must not open map admission.
- **CBSP split parser/rebase primitive (2026-10-21; host + R5900 compile, production callback open):** added [`ps2_cbsp_open_parts()`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/include/ps2_cbsp.h) for the fixed header, final image, and relocation rows in separate spans, plus [`ps2_cbsp_rebase_in_place()`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/include/ps2_cbsp.h) for the already-unpublished fixed BSP image. It preserves existing CBSP v1 structural/CRC/source-extent/row/domain checks; rejects overlapping parts and relocation scratch; requires destination identity; validates every CTAG string pointer and effective EE address before the first write; then applies the same two-domain relocations as the legacy full-buffer oracle. Tests in [`ps2_cbsp_main.c`](../tests/ps2_cbsp_main.c) compare split output byte-for-byte, reject bad final row, aliasing and bad tag strings without mutating staged image; [`test_cbsp.py`](../tests/test_cbsp.py) also runs the frozen Longest package (HP2 SHA-256 `6f9c19feb4df9e7ec49e797ce3eb6f7cfbdef3c89c6363b12aab2f05359f5a5b`, B000 image 374,784 bytes, 214 rows) through the C API and compares all output with Python. The subsequent `python3 tools/hcu_ps2_closure.py --layer all --no-bootstrap-memory` compiled **369 units, 0 failures**; final exact CBSP port source SHA-256 `4f317c775928b827412efc47611389f120629260e887897fc4e369660f40e10d`, object SHA-256 `22843db2ca61269827c9057d0315a0bf5a6fcb739b9d42f3e99a723de42ddc01`; split API header SHA-256 `0c8993aa05981f34482dc6e19b72e704a3e55c158fba7c048760eb85ed95597e`, direct C harness SHA-256 `7c996ce8261ee76083a516a7cfc6ddae99c59cf6702229cef68bff5d85af45d1`, Python test SHA-256 `59292e2318899136dcbd85c5e777b14bae242a4a263804ff7acf85e0e8afde03`; M0 remains open at 322 rooted externals. The next broader gameplay oracle is report-sequenced in [`m2-d20-gameplay-oracle-first-slice-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-d20-gameplay-oracle-first-slice-2026-10-21.md): build a separate host-only executable retaining actual CE `game_initialize()` → `game_load(D20)` → `game_unload()` first, with bound guarded spans and adapters limited to machine boundaries; then layer map initialization, authored actors, local player, real tick and CPU pose. The current scenario oracle does not include those gameplay modules, and the existing game-tick order fixture stubs phase behavior. The first attempt is source-closure measured in [`m2-d20-game-initialize-closure-blocker-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-d20-game-initialize-closure-blocker-2026-10-21.md): rooting actual initialization/load/unload/map-loaded query from the selected 370-object PS2 closure retains **279 unresolved live symbols** (512 names in the undefined table before relocation filtering). The new test passes only by asserting that precise blocker inventory, not by executing CE; it adds no success stubs and classifies live callers. Thus the next step is reviewed, bounded machine-boundary resolution—not treating the map-only host fixture as a gameplay engine. Keep production admission UI-only and treat Halo-64 only as an adapter separation comparator, not an AI/HS/effects-cut template. No PS2 capacity, D20 guest load, or visible rendering is implied.

**Step 11 — Preserve and verify CE tick order**

It asserts the local non-network phase trace including the first-person RNG lock bracket, normal 1/30 and double-speed 1/60 effect/antenna deltas, pre/post data-array and light-recovery checks, and identical per-tick state hash/RNG on repeated runs. `PYTHONPATH=. python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_hcu_game_tick_fixture.py` passes (**1 passed**). This is stronger than the prior source-order-only assertion but stubs all phase behavior and calls `game_tick()` directly; it does not exercise CE `game_time_update()` scheduling, loaded actors/objects, production loop dispatch, render scheduling, or a gameplay guest. Keep Step 11 partial and retain those gates.

Extract ordered call positions and fail if allegiance, units, AI, players, effects, first-person weapons, game engine/scripts, or objects are moved, omitted, or duplicated; also assert initialization establishes scenario/objects/players before the first tick.
- Add a minimal deterministic fixture that records tick number, phase entry, and state hash around a real CE tick. Verify exactly one simulation tick occurs per requested tick and that a render callback does not call back into `game_tick`; host source-order checks alone do not prove runtime scheduling.
- Preserve fixed tick delta and random-seed lock behavior used by first-person weapon updates. Record any PS2-only call-site adaptation with a `port:` rationale and ensure it does not change retail phase order.

**Step 12 — Publish pad samples at the CE tick boundary**

- **Frame-versus-tick correction:** The production shell polls once per frame, and CE `game_time_update()` can schedule zero or several simulation ticks after that poll. The previous “poll sequence equals tick sequence” criterion is invalid for CE and has been replaced with a frame-ID-to-tick-range trace requirement above. Existing host bridge tests prove pad conversion/frame-boundary ordering, not guest controller input or input semantics across catch-up ticks; `m3-2026-10-21-tick-order-fixture-review.md` records the source trace and exact distinction.

- Poll and publish once at CE’s input **frame** boundary, immediately before the device snapshot, then correlate that frame sample to the zero-or-more CE ticks scheduled within the same frame. `input_frame_begin()` runs once in `main_loop()`, whereas [`game_time_update()`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/game/game_time.c#L384-L493) may schedule zero or multiple `game_tick()` calls (capped at seven) from elapsed fixed-step time. Do not require poll-sequence equals tick-sequence or poll once per internal tick; both conflict with retail CE scheduling. Add frame ID plus first/last tick index to traces, prove each internal tick sees the intended CE input state, and test 0/1/multi-tick frames for no duplicate poll, stale sample, or lost edge. The poll-before-device-snapshot implementation is in [`input_xbox.c`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/input/input_xbox.c), parent publication in [`hcu_input_bridge.c`](../src/stream/hcu_input_bridge.c).
- For each of four logical players, test neutral→pressed→held→released transitions across consecutive ticks; disconnect/reconnect must clear stale buttons and analog values. Exercise both port/multitap slot arrangements, analog triggers, D-pad, deadzone boundary, and simultaneous button edges without passing through parent gameplay deadzone remapping.
- Add a guest trace containing player index, CE pressed/held/released masks, tick number, and controller-presence state. Host tests verify the bridge; PCSX2 pad injection or real controller evidence is separately recorded and must not be inferred from host fakes.

Add capacity/overflow behavior (reject or truncate only explicitly optional visual requests), generation checks against object deletion/reuse, and atomic snapshot swap so render never sees a half-built view.
- Test one through four local views at maximum supported objects/nodes, exact-capacity and one-over-capacity cases, alternating buffers, map unload invalidation, and zero additional allocation. Verify 16-byte VIF payload alignment/word count using the existing DMA packer without claiming it has been submitted to GS.
