#### Remake M4 — Replace duplicate M13 combat

**Step 17 — Build the exhaustive weapon/objective ledger**

- Import the exact M13 weapon and actor/vehicle/objective row set from [`plans/0.native-ps2-halo-hybrid-port.md`](0.native-ps2-halo-hybrid-port.md), preserving one row per canonical `weap` tag plus frag/plasma grenades, flag/ball, Hunter fuel rod, Sentinel beam, and each vehicle/turret mount origin. Record map/tag source identity and whether a path is present or absent in the supplied retail maps.
- For each row, identify CE tag definition fields and relevant source routines (`source/items/weapons.c`, `projectiles.c`, `objects/damage.c`, actor/vehicle firing code), parent comparison fixture, owner/origin, test setup, expected normalized trace, pool classes, and evidence hashes. Add explicit columns for cadence, ammo/heat/charge/reload, projectile/material response, damage/region/shield/death, effects/audio requests, ownership/filtering, and presentation markers.
- Gate status individually: `unassessed`, `source-audited`, `host-parity`, `guest-interpreter`, `guest-recompiler`, or `accepted`. A row cannot advance because another weapon in its family passes; sphere diagnostics remain labeled smoke and absent tag paths remain documented rather than silently omitted.

**Step 18 — Verify projectile/effect exhaustion isolation**

- Fill projectile and effect pools independently to their declared capacities, snapshot all unrelated object datums, RNG/tick state, owner handles, and arena cursors, then attempt one additional allocation. Require failure to be local to that shot/effect and leave all snapshots unchanged; repeat with a free slot created by deletion to prove reuse and generation increment.
- Exercise simultaneous projectile impacts/explosions in deterministic handle order, including two blasts affecting the same shield/body, owner deletion before detonation, self/owner filtering, and area-damage query overflow. Assert each victim receives the CE-expected number/order of damage applications and no stale datum is mutated.
- Bind these tests to the actual HCU object/projectile/effect pool allocation routines and their tagged limits. Report bytes per datum, metadata, alignment, peak simultaneous count, and failure policy against the 2,621,440-byte `dynamic_simulation` region; no fallback heap allocation is allowed.

**Step 19 — Implement and prove the special-weapon CE paths**

- Fuel rod: source the weapon/projectile/effect tag values and CE trigger/projectile/damage routines; verify magazine/round decrement, cadence, projectile origin/owner, air-vs-water velocity/drag, collision material response, arming/fuse, detonation radius, line-of-sight/owner filters, simultaneous AoE, and effect/audio requests. The missing first-person graph affects presentation only; it must not cause a fabricated generic weapon profile. Remove the parent bespoke fuel-rod profile/viewmodel only after CE guest traces pass and its source can no longer enter the production target.
- Energy sword: exercise melee arbitration against weapon reload/put-away, lunge acquisition/range/line-of-sight, target movement during lunge, shield/body region damage, simultaneous attacker arbitration, cooldown and animation-event timing. Verify HCU owns accepted melee state and halo-ps2 consumes the resulting pose/effect snapshot; compare the hand/sword presentation separately from hit semantics.
- Gravity rifle: follow the tagged flame-projectile path but keep weapon identity, projectile ownership, cadence/ammo/heat, collision and damage/effects distinct from flamethrower. Verify projectile lifetime, contact/area response, and effects from its own tag data; do not route through a generic flamethrower shortcut.
- Each weapon test must cite its exact CE source functions and tag hash, run deterministic host traces, and later run interpreter/recompiler guest traces. A host-only pass does not permit deleting the parent implementation.

**Step 20 — Qualify integrated combat traces and budgets**

- For each ledger row marked ready, replay the same seed, map/BSP hash, tick-indexed input stream, spawn identities, and action sequence in interpreter and recompiler. Canonically sort object/projectile datums by stable handle+generation, serialize only gameplay state (positions/velocities, weapon state/ammo/heat, projectile ownership/fuse, health/shields/death, RNG, tick), and hash each tick so the first divergent frame is identifiable.
- Include actor and vehicle firing origins, objectives, grenades, all supported weapon rows, area damage, pool exhaustion, and concurrent explosions. Test zero/one/multiple render publications between simulation ticks and require identical combat hashes, proving rendering cannot perturb random state or cadence.
- Capture region high-water and exhaustion counters under a worst-case simultaneous combat fixture; verify `dynamic_simulation` and `frame_scratch` fit their budgets and the 5,767,168-byte reserve is untouched. Preserve per-mode ELF/settings/source hashes, logs, traces, and test result artifacts. M4 closes only when every ledger row is accepted on both guest modes; a partial weapon subset or parent `WORLD_PLAY` smoke remains insufficient.

## Assumptions and acceptance boundary

M2 does not need a drawn scene. It needs a real UI scenario load and one gameplay-map load whose measured bytes fit, or an explicit stop when they do not. M3 does not retire parent collision until the traces match. M4 does not inherit the parent play ELF’s partial weapon smoke. Physical OPL, optical media, and a retail frame remain outside these three exits. The reserve stays unspent.

### Step 7 multiplayer-bot fog behavior — unresolved, not a proven nonzero defect

Do not implement an inferred formula for CE `scenario_fog_at_point()`: both the CE body and Halo-64 body are zero-return stubs ([CE `scenario.c:803-809`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/scenario/scenario.c#L803), [Halo-64 `scenario.c:780-786`](../submodules/halo-64/source/scenario/scenario.c#L780)). CE establishes only the AI use/thresholds ([`ai.c:2363-2372`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/ai/ai.c#L2363), [`actor_perception.c:2484-2503`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/ai/actor_perception.c#L2484)); the fog schema identifies density/distance/depth/plane fields ([`tag_schema_render.c:911-929`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/linux/game/tag_schema_render.c#L911)) but not a viewer-to-target integration formula. This establishes an AI callsite, not that nonzero attenuation is necessary or that the current zero is wrong. The supplied Xbox XBE is still unmapped at this function; REA's raw-text address probe is not a valid source-coordinate match. A separate PC build-2342 decomp also returns zero, but is not Xbox-retail authority. Therefore retain the current zero and treat exact Xbox bot-fog parity as an unresolved question; do not count it as a demonstrated nonzero Step 7 defect by itself. If the retail routine or controlled outputs later prove a nonzero effect, implement and test that behavior. Detailed evidence and limits: [`m2-step7-retail-xbe-fog-rea-feasibility-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-retail-xbe-fog-rea-feasibility-2026-10-22.md); supplemental consumer context: [`step7-env-sound-consumers-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/step7-env-sound-consumers-2026-10-21.md).

**REA feasibility + provenance follow-up (2026-10-22):** REA rejects the supplied XISO and XBE containers as unsupported formats. The prior and current extracted XBE container SHA-256 values differ (`6aeb…` vs `4094…`), but the current file is byte-identical to the prior file over its complete length, both describe the same 24 sections, and their complete `.text` ranges (`file 0x1000..0x16FE40`, VA `0x11000`) compare equal to the retained REA raw input SHA-256 `e263c4e8…`. Thus the older stripped-code dossier is valid evidence about the executable code in the current supplied ISO; the discrepancy is trailing-container bytes, not `.text`. It still does **not** establish bot-fog behavior: the 2,355 stripped procedures are unnamed, and the previously inspected `FUN_00015bfd` has no demonstrated relation to fog. Do not infer/implement the missing formula. Detailed source-byte comparison and limits are in [`m2-step7-retail-xbe-fog-rea-feasibility-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-retail-xbe-fog-rea-feasibility-2026-10-22.md), updated after the provenance check. The only authoritative next semantic evidence remains controlled retail outputs or a defensibly identified routine.

### Step 8 texture/map repartition is not yet justified (2026-10-22)

**Diagnostic arena high-water primitive added (2026-10-22; host test passed):** HCU's PS2 allocator now exposes opt-in capacity/current/high-water/failure counters under `HALO_PS2_STEP8_MEMORY_DIAGNOSTIC`; ordinary builds add no telemetry API or counter storage. The fixture verifies the preboot SDK fallback stays outside the metric, 16-byte alignment, monotonic 48-byte peak, exhaustion/overflow counts, and free/rebind reset. Reproduce with `python3 tools/test_hcu_ps2_malloc_diagnostic.py`. Implementation: [`physical_memory_map.h:48-61`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/cache/physical_memory_map.h#L48), [`ps2_memory.c:312-367`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_memory.c#L312), test [`hcu_ps2_malloc_diagnostic_main.c`](../tests/hcu_ps2_malloc_diagnostic_main.c), report [`m2-step8-malloc-arena-telemetry-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step8-malloc-arena-telemetry-2026-10-22.md). This measures only compatibility `malloc` mapped to `dynamic_simulation`; game-state object pools/AI arrays remain separate and live-match EE high-water is still missing. The R5900 compiler is not installed here, so target compile/runtime evidence remains required.

**Step 8 MP dynamic-pool ownership audit (2026-10-22; source-backed, runtime high-water still open):** the 2,621,440-byte `dynamic_simulation` region is bound to HCU's 16-byte-aligned monotonic compatibility allocator, but CE's 1 MiB object memory pool and actor/prop/swarm datum arrays currently allocate inside `resident_gameplay` through `game_state_malloc()` / `game_state_data_new()`. Do not double-count those bytes against `dynamic_simulation`; equally, do not infer that the remaining arena fits live match demand because no combined peak counter or 4-player/bot/co-op fixture exists. PS2 count limits (2,048 object slots, 256 actors, 768 props, 32 swarms, 1,024 particles, etc.) are caps, not byte measurements. Report [`mp-step8-dynamic-pool-pressure-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/mp-step8-dynamic-pool-pressure-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `5e92c580a3b7e0faf4ea7d702ca4c3f2c9f7eb7b546c0bb2817532f5d595a1cf`; implementation boundaries: [`hcu_memory_bind.c:19-67`](../src/stream/hcu_memory_bind.c#L19), [`ps2_memory.c:288-331`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_memory.c#L288), [`objects.c:1040-1063`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/objects/objects.c#L1040), [`actors.c:560-577`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/ai/actors.c#L560). Required next evidence is live allocator/pool high-water and exhaustion/reload behavior under the supported maximum player/bot/co-op and weapon/effect workload on all supported maps; Step 8 remains open.

**Step 8 owner telemetry primitive (2026-10-22; opt-in host fixture + R5900 object compile):** separate `HALO_PS2_STEP8_MEMORY_DIAGNOSTIC` snapshots now account for the game-state CPU cursor and the CE object-pool cursor, high-water, and allocation failures. The object pool is nested in game-state backing; do not sum the two. The independent compatibility malloc arena remains the `dynamic_simulation` owner and is not changed by this fixture. `python3 tools/test_hcu_step8_memory_owners.py` passes the source-extracted host test for 64-byte game-state/object-pool owners, one rejection in each, restart/reset, and unchanged dynamic-arena sentinel. Both modified C units compile with R5900 GCC 15.2.0 in normal and opt-in profiles; normal objects have no diagnostic symbols/counters, while these two diagnostic objects add 620 B `.text` and 24 B `.bss` combined. Report [`m2-step8-game-state-object-pool-telemetry-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step8-game-state-object-pool-telemetry-2026-10-22.md), which records exact source/test hashes and limits. This is instrumentation plumbing, not a gameplay workload, map/renderer combined peak, PCSX2 or retail target runtime result; no 1–4 player, bot or co-op capacity claim follows. Step 8 remains open.

**Step 8 workload-fixture feasibility (2026-10-22; source trace + target link):** the current Step 8 PCSX2 profile runs after global `game_initialize()` but its 13-map loop calls `scenario_load()`/`scenario_unload()` directly and prints `query_only=1`; the `HALO_PS2_M2_UI_LIFECYCLE_TEST` entry then intentionally loops instead of entering CE's main game loop. It never invokes `game_initialize_for_new_map()`, local player creation, `objects_place()`/`ai_place()`, or frame simulation. The normal CE bridge is `main_new_map()` (`static` in `main.c`), which runs `game_load`, per-map initialization, local player creation, start/pulse; a new isolated profile needs a test-only bridge to that function and assertions on actual players/units/owner snapshots. `ai_place()` only creates scenario-authored encounters; no configurable MP bot spawner is wired to this diagnostic, so do not claim bot coverage until the bot meaning/spawn path and workload are explicit. Build reproduction `python3 tools/hcu_parent_link_probe.py --step8-mp-capacity-diagnostic` linked the gameplay machine with zero undefined imports but remains `acceptance=false`; its limits explicitly exclude guest map transaction. Report [`m2-step8-gameplay-workload-fixture-feasibility-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step8-gameplay-workload-fixture-feasibility-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `e75011428d4846b6a368aa5d3e8402ad53085bffeb8d9c61c07ea6138e5a54cc`. Next: bridge to CE `main_new_map()`, qualify one local-player map/tick/teardown, extend to the supported local-player modes and all 13 maps, then separately implement/identify real bot spawning. No production budget changed; Step 8 remains open.

**Provider-bound combined MP fixture refresh (2026-10-22; native ILP32):** reran the provider-layout candidate across all 13 stock multiplayer maps. The retained executable binds candidate tag/BSP/texture/audio/dynamic/claims spans through HCU's real provider, then performs two CE load/query/unload cycles per map while retaining decoded `TEXS`, `LMn`, and selected `OTEX`; all 26 rows passed. **Step 7 multiplayer detail-object consumer (2026-10-22; host CPU path):** [`tests/test_hcu_mp_detail_object_consumer.py`](../tests/test_hcu_mp_detail_object_consumer.py) extracts HCU's actual camera-cell selector and exercises a synthetic valid-data branch plus the source-derived detail roots for all 13 stock multiplayer maps. Retain the BSP fields for custom-map compatibility; broader Step 7 and Step 8 gates remain open.

**Step 7 multiplayer lens-flare marker consumer (2026-10-22; host CPU selector):** [`tests/test_hcu_mp_lens_flare_all_maps.py`](../tests/test_hcu_mp_lens_flare_all_maps.py) compiles CE's actual `rasterizer_lens_flare_submit_for_cluster()` and compares the complete ordered marker-submit sequence against independent raw BSP data for all 13 stock multiplayer maps. Halo-PS2 documents its own sprite VU1 output for lens flares; Halo-64's 3D-widget draw stub is only a reduced N64 comparison, not PS2 guidance. Step 7/8 remain open.

The topology review's initial 170,176-byte transfer estimate is superseded as a *candidate geometry* by the relocation-tail-inclusive profile below, which takes 179,368 bytes from `texture_cache` into the combined tag/BSP span; both profiles remain diagnostic-only. The all-13 static pack inventory now measures the maximum per-map `TEXS + selected OTEX + LMn` baseline at 2,144,640 B (Blood Gulch), but it does not measure runtime texture-cache occupancy, alignment/fragmentation, decoded staging overlap, render visibility, object/bot/equipment/effect assets, or residency across 1–4 players, bots, and co-op ([`m2-step8-mp-static-render-asset-inventory-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step8-mp-static-render-asset-inventory-2026-10-22.md)). The candidate CE lifecycle fixture protects but does not populate its smaller 4,014,936-byte texture remainder. CE binds the texture owner and its cache/Bink consumers; parent EE upload staging and GS eDRAM are separate resources, so neither section totals nor GS residency establish transferable EE bytes. Keep the production memory split, reserve, and map-admission gate unchanged until actual renderer texture loads, lifetimes, and high-water are measured across the full MP envelope. Evidence: [`m2-mp-step8-topology-solution-review-2026-10-21.md`](../docs/reports/m2-mp-step8-topology-solution-review-2026-10-21.md), including its 2026-10-22 texture-owner follow-up; owner source boundaries are [`hcu_main.c:218-236`](../src/stream/hcu_main.c#L218), [`ps2_memory.c:27,158-164`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/core/ps2_memory.c#L27), and [`run_world_viewer.py`](../tools/run_world_viewer.py). No capacity credit is taken from campaign traces or Halo-64's N64 reductions; both are outside this multiplayer acceptance workload.

**Texture-owner runtime follow-up (2026-10-22; host only):** the candidate texture remainder is no longer merely protected/empty: [`test_halo_ps2_mp_texture_runtime.py`](../tools/test_halo_ps2_mp_texture_runtime.py) runs halo-ps2's actual HP2 section loader and TEXS/LMAP parsers for all 13 source-pinned stock maps, retaining decoded TEXS, every emitted LM section, and scenario-selected OTEX in the proposed 4,014,936-byte owner. ### Step 7 all-13 multiplayer weather-palette bounds (2026-10-22)

Added [`audit_retail_mp_weather_palette.py`](../tools/audit_retail_mp_weather_palette.py) and its hash-pinned report [`m2-step7-mp-weather-palette-inventory-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-mp-weather-palette-inventory-2026-10-22.md). Reproduction: `python3 tools/audit_retail_mp_weather_palette.py`. It reads raw decompressed source BSPs for all 13 stock MP maps, validates palette and cluster block extents, checks every cluster weather index, and compares each BSP's palette count with CE's 32 `wind_states` entries in [`wind.c:59-69,121,188-211`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/scenario/wind.c#L59). Result: 13 maps / 13 BSPs, max 2 entries (Damnation), 239 clusters, 235 valid index-0 references, 4 `NONE` references in Carousel, zero malformed references or capacity violations. Script SHA-256 `6bb70a654318d7c68b14379dae5894e29aa5fdf68be90b6ed3e42e02abc3db61`; report SHA-256 `601c0eb50b6598b7bfee5fc985cefee56be7357fe73af8f9d593bd6af8fa79b0`. This closes only the raw-source structural/capacity subcheck. The package-backed Damnation fixture passes natively outside the sandbox: `python3 -m pytest -q -s tests/test_hcu_mp_wind_package.py` → **1 passed** (independently rerun: 7.94 s; final agent rerun: 8.03 s). It mounts the full HP2 package through the production map/CTAG/CBSP path, checks relocated palette/tag/cluster records against raw retail bytes, runs real CE `wind_initialize_for_new_map()`, `wind_update()`, and current queries, verifies the `NONE` entry produces zero, and verifies stable output after dispose/reinitialize. It required fixture-only linkage to the CE inline spline alias and the same real CE geosphere/math closure as the existing wind-consumer test; no production wind behavior or oracle values were bypassed/changed. **Step 8 capacity floor blocker (2026-10-22):** Added [`audit_mp_tag_bsp_capacity_floor.py`](../tools/audit_mp_tag_bsp_capacity_floor.py) and [`m2-step8-mp-tag-bsp-capacity-floor-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step8-mp-tag-bsp-capacity-floor-2026-10-22.md). It decodes all 13 schema-built HP2 packages and counts the exact resident CTAG image plus the largest decoded CBSP image and relocation rows (the production streamer stores the rows in the BSP-cache tail). The production default split is 2 MiB for tags in a 6 MiB `current_bsp` ([`hcu_main.c:28-30`](../src/stream/hcu_main.c#L28), [`hcu_memory_bind.c:43-56`](../src/stream/hcu_memory_bind.c#L43)); every MP CTAG image is 4.316–4.556 MiB, so the present split cannot load any of the 13. More strongly, Sidewinder's CTAG+BSP+relocation floor is **6,449,848 B**, exceeding the whole arena by **158,392 B**, before claims/alignment/metadata. This proves split adjustment alone cannot fit Sidewinder. Audit command and exact artifact hashes are in the report; generated JSON SHA-256 `a9d147ba32c6ab503eca773fa42db36302261f5afb6712948bfe989a51b91b55`. This is a static lower bound, not target high-water. Step 8 stays open; do not spend reserve or change budgets from this alone. It directs the next capacity work toward a measured lossless representation/placement change and confirms why host hit fixtures with 16/8 MiB caches are not target-sized query evidence.

**Capacity-floor correction, same date:** review of the envelope implementation caught that HP2 `CTAG.raw_size` includes the 52-byte CTAG header and its relocation list; only the decoded `image_size` resides in `tag_cache`, while CTAG relocations are separately charged to the map-owner allocator ([`ps2_cache.c:655-758`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_cache.c#L655), [`ctag_mount.c:396-433`](../src/stream/ctag_mount.c#L396)). The corrected audit reports CTAG images **4,241,088–4,477,116 B**, still all over the current 2 MiB split. Sidewinder's CTAG image + decoded B000 + CBSP relocation tail is **6,371,864 B**, exceeding `current_bsp` by **80,408 B**; its separate CTAG relocation scratch is 77,932 B. Corrected tool/JSON SHA-256: `a49ea9825c9af93cd352abc03ed91e3c60549f98f8c783f12291b39b15839201` / `5130b6332f6e395481f70ea1445a3ac161845dcaa5fae228abac0a5bb788d19a`. This correction supersedes the preceding paragraph's numeric values; the conclusions (all present per-map tag-cache splits fail, Sidewinder exceeds the whole arena floor, no target-capacity pass) are unchanged. Full corrected table and owner boundaries are in the linked report.

**Native target-capacity preflight/rollback check (2026-10-22):** implemented an ILP32 test driver that calls the real CE `ps2_cache_preflight_map()` → HCU `hcu_map_service_preflight()` path for all 13 package-pinned multiplayer maps twice with the production 2 MiB tag cache, 4 MiB BSP cache, 1 MiB map owner, 512 KiB decode scratch, and 786,432-byte claims buffer. Keep Step 7 and Step 8 open and retain this profile as diagnostic until those broader capacity and behavior gates pass.

**Step 7 all-13 multiplayer weather/wind consumer execution (2026-10-22):** the package-backed fixture now runs the actual CE `wind_initialize_for_new_map()`, `wind_update()`, and `scenario_get_current_from_weather_palette()` for normal/simple/damped and cluster-selected queries on all 13 stock multiplayer BSPs, with source-derived palette/tag/cluster records and two load/unload cycles per map. An initial oracle failure caught a field-order bug in the independent expected-value model: CE embeds `real_euler_angles2d` as `{ yaw, pitch }` ([`real_math.h:396-401`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/math/real_math.h#L396)), so the expected model was corrected to match the declared tag order; the CE implementation was not changed. The corrected elevated native ILP32 binary exited 0 and emitted **26/26** weather-query and **26/26** map-cycle pass rows. Exact artifacts: report [`m2-step7-mp-weather-wind-13-map-host-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-mp-weather-wind-13-map-host-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `68503a9d5ba605586707f202f9052fa5a7754a6a97e5e53302caa2708c7a31ab`; ELF `ecc096846afaa6e22328f4b7c5e49c326add527e8fcd1318a08321b3c095fc55`; link map `9490fc66f329fe27f3ae726168121b2bf21c9c62326d284da655640fb95d7192`; retained log [`native-run-final.log`](../build/m2-mp-weather-wind-13/native-run-final.log), SHA-256 `7992858a40f0d65ae455b0bb083e5715df4769e261cc24c5ebb7f3369b3f3ccc`; source-derived golden `fa333f74b40bcde3359ab51f169c49ef26056e1496e0a293d0c86d5a4fbf0404`. This closes the all-stock-MP host wind/weather state-and-query subcheck only. The fixture uses host-sized backing arrays; it does not validate weather-particle presentation/audio, target arena capacity, EE timing, or a guest map load, and Step 7/8 remain open.

**Step 7 all-13 multiplayer fog-region consumer execution (2026-10-22):** the raw-source-driven native ILP32 fixture now invokes CE's actual `scenario_get_fog_region_index()` on relocated BSP/tag data for one supported case per each of the 13 stock MP maps over two load/unload cycles. Beaver Creek (103), Chill Out (107), and Damnation (108) each have a validated plane→region→palette/tag chain; the other ten have a source-verified cluster `fog_reference == NONE`. The 6 plane rows check both sides of the plane, and the 20 NONE rows check null-position and concrete-position queries; map publication remains stable. **Step 8 co-resident multiplayer map + texture owner diagnostic (2026-10-22; candidate profile only):** an isolated native ILP32 fixture loads each stock MP map through CE's real tag/BSP lifecycle, retains those spans while halo-ps2's actual section loader loads decoded `TEXS`, every emitted `LMn`, and scenario-selected `OTEX` into an adjacent candidate texture owner, then runs the existing CE collision/PVS/environment/lifecycle queries before unload. All **26/26** map/cycle rows passed, including 26 deliberate corrupt-CRC failures with cursor rollback. The largest normal texture-owner high-water was Blood Gulch **2,144,680 / 4,014,936 B**; its failure-path transient peaked at **2,550,728 B**; shared scratch peaked at **98,352 / 524,288 B**. The diagnostic topology is 4,477,120 B tags + 1,993,704 B BSP/relocation tail + 4,014,936 B candidate texture owner (= 10,485,760 B), plus separately measured 1 MiB map owner and 512 KiB scratch. Reproduction, per-map table, source pins and limits are in [`mp-step8-combined-map-texture-owner-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/mp-step8-combined-map-texture-owner-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `44887cd04855b481d11b36566b913cce788599f9d7096d1c6aca959cc1f71aef`; exact candidate ELF `e6eda0a1686547cac81991741c986a9d32571fd8290c8cf7fe8c7c73ffe0d376`; map `683d9f9dd86f5002f884ef5800832a444552bfca196060c875f40acef46263e4`; complete log `5181f12a655f1650176e36d281774761009915193ba3a950148e166d5ac6c431`; validated manifest `12406d4423f1ff1c7ce5769a454b7fa353953374ea9c979b5821f5a4d6f98e70`. The actual fixture injection/section load are [`hcu_mp_capacity_main.c:391,400`](../tests/hcu_mp_capacity_main.c#L391) and [`mp_texture_candidate_owner.c:44-106`](../tests/mp_texture_candidate_owner.c#L44); loader behavior is [`section.c:153,199-243`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/section.c#L153). This demonstrates candidate co-residency for selected asset classes, not production admission or full match-time residency: object packs, custom weapons, animation/effects/HUD, GS uploads, eviction/in-flight frame lifetime, EE physical placement and target timing remain unmeasured. **Historical provider-alignment caveat (superseded by the two later pad8/provider entries):** the tested immediate tag+BSP→texture boundary is 8 mod 16 because the relocation-tail BSP span is 1,993,704 B, while `physical_memory_bind_*` requires 16-byte-aligned bases ([`ps2_memory.c:86-108`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/port/ps2/src/ps2_memory.c#L86)). The initial unpadded fixture did not prove provider binding; the pad8 provider-binding and same-process all-13 integrated host results are recorded in later entries below. Keep Step 8 open and do not alter production budgets from this host diagnostic.

**Step 7 exact shared-edge tie (2026-10-22; CE runtime, host ILP32):** a separate bounded raw-source search found a true exact-midpoint first-contact tie on Beaver Creek between same-material surfaces 39/94. The independent inclusive-polygon oracle requires both faces in the simultaneous-contact set; the CE-source-equivalent BSP2D routes permit both; real CE `collision_test_vector()` deterministically selects surface 39, with material/type, leaf and hit-parameter assertions on both load cycles. The scan covered one BSP from each of the 13 stock MP maps with same-material, unflagged adjacent pairs, shared endpoints, ray along the lower-index face normal, empty→solid BSP3D route, and at least one serialized BSP2D face route; 11 maps had a qualifying tie under these constraints, while Boarding Action and Damnation had no candidate under this bounded search. This does not imply they lack ties under all possible rays. Report [`m2-step7-mp-exact-edge-tie-ce-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-mp-exact-edge-tie-ce-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `165d6d99bc2d69715ad3267aafd2979f19241b7e317f61de34d322d90ebc9bdf`; run manifest `df6707d9700e451454d4ce06f02e43cec2d8ce0237ff04d1ef49fd33022928f0`; golden `d447ea5f8ec891c9d8deed5361101f3b24c7bbd867c2d709d1aaae53ff7a333d`; executable `4eabf55a6b2d0a1fbb0676557b2437309c96d52f9653bcbff4dcd2d38663764a`; map `16b24885b93045ca47b1c8fb61baec4d212b3f7df9645aad8ed5e19773b049f9`; native log `0af35b0ce5f3224512118a527190b2d207e93a4c1755111644b07d191fe42739`. Exact-edge behavior now has an accepted stock-MP case; a separate exact-vertex CE case is recorded immediately below. Remaining collision work is additional topology, consumer, and target-capacity coverage—not the absence of any edge/vertex example.

**Step 7 exact shared-vertex tie (2026-10-22; CE runtime, host ILP32):** the raw-source search found a Beaver Creek exact-vertex first-contact case with six simultaneous geometric faces `{39,75,162,315,549,684}`; independently traversed source BSP2D routes admit `{39,162,315,549}`. **Step 8 provider-aligned candidate region-binding diagnostic (2026-10-22; host ILP32, supersedes the unpadded candidate dimensions above):** the revised 8-byte boundary pad keeps all candidate bases 16-byte aligned without changing the combined nominal 10 MiB total: tag 4,477,120 B; BSP 1,993,712 B; texture 4,014,928 B; claims 808,856 B. The actual HCU PS2 provider accepted disjoint spans and `physical_memory_ready()` passed; malformed misaligned binding and outer-canary checks also passed. Reproduction report [`mp-step8-candidate-region-adjacency-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/mp-step8-candidate-region-adjacency-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `aa568de5364811581e3f093e4a15709fea82d99844f5037bad8050452e2cfd5c`; binder-test manifest `44528eac0e25e383544d60fcd34372a33f1bdd3dc7a5642b87f140512fbe1afe`; binder ELF `64a5d48829e2a62530abb4bec85100c43df7f7b225db49491b878a0f7d321e9f`. Independently reran the pad8 combined-section verifier: **26/26** map/query/texture cycles passed; max normal texture high-water 2,144,672/4,014,928 B, corrupt-CRC transient peak 2,550,720 B, scratch 98,352/524,288 B. Combined ELF `3587b9b7313471bcbcc5ac82c4c4a8c2e86a662f452bdc31d55c31e8d7a37274`; link map `4fee4facb914362c55fc8a3263b987e130027c32e9e0738f630c67bb20d5f3d7`; full log `e07d4674c42c31555cc1073e1c5a81971d42a35e18e59a084f98484d19429905`; manifest `cee281799eba3a522a424880bb4e7f0d588ce378886db202e2a80e134062e38d`. The key remaining implementation boundary is real: the current guarded layout puts a 16-byte canary between `current_bsp` and `texture_cache`; the combined map owner crosses it and borrows 179,360 B from texture ([`mem.c:110-143`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/core/mem.c#L110), [`hcu_memory_bind.c:41-62`](../src/stream/hcu_memory_bind.c#L41)). Therefore provider feasibility plus a synthetic contiguous backing does not prove the current production region layout/binder can own it. Next is a layout variant with one guard at the combined owner boundary and explicit ownership/cursor accounting, then the same all-13 CE map+texture matrix; do not change production caps/admission before that test and full live asset/target work. This closes only API alignment/provider feasibility, not Step 8.
**Step 8 guarded candidate-layout/provider proof (2026-10-22; isolated host ILP32 diagnostic):** a test-only topology now places one combined 10,485,760 B owner at the production `current_bsp` offset, with aligned 4,477,120 B tag, 1,993,712 B BSP, and 4,014,928 B texture slices; it preserves separate guards at the combined owner boundary and around other regions. The actual HCU PS2 memory provider accepted the spans; readiness, game-state/claim ownership, mark rollback, misaligned-bind rejection, and all nine canaries passed. Exact reproduction: `python3 tools/test_hcu_mp_candidate_region_bind.py` (native ILP32 required; sandbox SIGSYS), output `candidate-guarded-layout combined_off=4701248 combined_bytes=10485760 tag=4477120 bsp=1993712 texture=4014928 guards=9 marks=zero aligned=16 status=passed`. Report [`mp-step8-candidate-region-adjacency-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/mp-step8-candidate-region-adjacency-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `4eaaa8faf8e5dc04bf824589a311a1c3b2649f40ac290e90c3aa0867d50bb9ae`; manifest `d9f8cf7d756f9398029151d68a080bc46e342080801a9842765fd0b1bff56183`; test ELF `f0a5fb31fd4ad780e3afb4e479025bf4c3e8e279d3cc93e0dca6ad9509f6ebf0`; map `2b255a9d7afbb1a64d8c36adbec6da17307ab97cc0cefed18291f993e9aff5a9`; log `49c35be181447ce156293b872dcb30e35601a8eb31b7626cf8db770817610926`. Together with the separate 26/26 co-resident map+texture matrix, this proves the candidate topology is provider-bindable and supports selected asset/query paths under host buffers. It does not prove both paths in one run, production v2 ownership integration, complete match-time assets, GS upload/pin lifetimes, physical EE placement, or target timing; keep map admission and production budgets unchanged.

**Step 7 bot-fog unresolved source-backed gap (2026-10-22):** CE `scenario_fog_at_point()` and Halo-64 both return constant zero, while CE AI callers use the value in actor perception. A zero golden would only freeze a known stub. The retail ISO’s exact `.text` bytes were opened through REA as raw x86 at XBE VA `0x11000`; Ghidra found 2,355 unnamed procedures and no strings, but the XBE-to-source correspondence needed to identify the function/data is still absent. Therefore do not implement an inferred attenuation formula. Next authoritative evidence is controlled retail CE output vectors or a defensible executable/source mapping. Details: [`m2-step7-mp-bot-fog-attenuation-blocker-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-mp-bot-fog-attenuation-blocker-2026-10-22.md) and updated [`m2-step7-retail-xbe-fog-rea-feasibility-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-retail-xbe-fog-rea-feasibility-2026-10-22.md).

The code boundary is [`scenario.c:803-809`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/scenario/scenario.c#L803), with CE actor-fog consumers in [`ai.c:2363-2372`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/ai/ai.c#L2363) and [`actor_perception.c:2484-2503`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/ai/actor_perception.c#L2484); the independent blocker report SHA-256 is `db363ac1199986eb12534d61a2f507cea749ed9c7a4ba8a6dec7cce6861b77f5`. Updated REA feasibility report SHA-256: `7b04a7020628a704b998b19c7245208f91f4f8c0540c046e2038cec9a59ac34b`.

**Step 7 Prisoner CE breakable damage/reset transition (2026-10-22; host ILP32; supersedes the earlier bitmap-only breakable toggle):** [`test_hcu_mp_breakable_runtime.py`](../tests/test_hcu_mp_breakable_runtime.py) now composes the real CE `breakable_surface_damage()`, `breakable_surfaces_reset()`, `breakable_surface_extant()`, and published-map `collision_test_vector()` against Prisoner's source-derived hit on surface 457 / breakable index 1. **Step 8 all-13 co-resident render-owner measurement (2026-10-22; independent rerun):** I reran [`tools/test_halo_ps2_mp_render_owner.py`](../tools/test_halo_ps2_mp_render_owner.py), which converts the pinned MP maps and uses halo-ps2’s actual [`section.c`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/section.c#L153), [`tex.c`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/tex.c#L24), and [`model.c`](../submodules/halo-ps2/engine/src/world/model.c) loaders to hold `TEXS` + all `LMn` + scenario `MODL` + `OTEX` in one measured owner per map. **Step 7 REA address-map refinement (2026-10-22):** CE's source symbol header places `_scenario_fog_at_point` at `0x17DEE0`, but that address in the raw XBE text session resolves to an instruction inside `FUN_0017de80` (`PUSH ECX` at the queried byte), not a function entry. This proves direct address transfer from the decomp listing to the Xbox ISO is invalid, not that the XBE lacks the routine. The PC build-2342 comparator returns zero, but is not Xbox authority. Preserve the current zero and keep exact Xbox parity unresolved; nonzero attenuation is not demonstrated as necessary, so this is not by itself a proven Step 7 defect. The REA digest and limitations are recorded in [`m2-step7-retail-xbe-fog-rea-feasibility-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-retail-xbe-fog-rea-feasibility-2026-10-22.md).

**Step 7/8 all-13 target-side CE A* and bounded path-state allocation (2026-10-22; PCSX2 recompiler):** extended the existing Step 8 candidate-profile guest loop to run unchanged CE `path_state_find()` against each loaded, relocated MP BSP. The fixture derives start/goal points from bounded collision-surface edge rings, runs the exact source CE `path_input_new()`/`path_state_new()`/`path_state_destination()`/`path_state_find()` entrypoints, and validates the returned hash entry plus a strictly depth-decreasing parent chain rooted at the requested start surface. It keeps the 82,060-byte `path_state` off the EE stack by using the PS2 bounded allocator and records allocation deltas, high-water, capacity, and failed-allocation count. The first run caught two real harness issues and was not accepted: full `path_state_build_path()` requires live actor identity for obstacle smoothing, so this isolated test now stops after A* graph search; and the allocator telemetry API had been compiled only into the caller, not `ps2_memory.c`, then accidentally emitted as a generated stub. The diagnostic build now enables telemetry on the allocator object and excludes the real provider from generated placeholders ([`hcu_ps2_closure.py:538-553`](../tools/hcu_ps2_closure.py#L538), [`hcu_ps2_link.py:439-454`](../tools/hcu_ps2_link.py#L439)). The successful current ELF `3f8efe1407f44a1f74d3a5f0d8c7c8941173d19f54ef17ffef258ce320ff93e3` passed `APPIMAGE_EXTRACT_AND_RUN=1 python3 tools/test_hcu_step7_path_guest.py --mode recompiler --timeout 600`: all 13 exact map/route pairs found the goal and rooted at their expected start, with zero allocator failures. The final monotonic allocator high-water was 2,122,976 / 2,621,440 B (498,464 B remaining); per-route results are bound in [`step7-path-validation.json`](../build/hcu-ps2-closure/emulator-m1-recompiler-80c299fa2b81415889d742d698a8c116/step7-path-validation.json), SHA-256 `aa1a711dfef03de8e09d7f6337d25050eb949d2355501eb56978b254bbdad3e2`, and full source/run limits in [`m2-step7-mp-target-astar-2026-10-22.md`](../docs/reports/m2-step7-mp-target-astar-2026-10-22.md), SHA-256 `70604486bd79f74af1c704e09a58fce45654319e4fc6825a687b43bfa7f09c88`. Runtime result/log SHA-256: `3e118a0f693b0737aa20f781ad300ca8f2ce211f7f77115425e223a986628e42` / `5ddc2b0bb5b4be8795fa5eadcc92cf69db42e41f0837257f14bf085177158fd1`. This closes only all-13 target CE A* route-search and diagnostic scratch-capacity evidence. The monotonic allocator deliberately accumulates its path-state allocations across this test loop; this is not an actor concurrency/live-match peak. `path_state_build_path()` obstacle smoothing, actor goal assignment/steering, bots/co-op workload, full rendering/asset residency, physical-EE timing, remaining BSP consumer/failure-atomicity checks, and production map admission remain open. Halo-64 is comparative only, not a PS2 budget/parity authority; REA was not useful for this source-level/runtime call-path question.

**Step 7 retail BSP transaction fixture ABI correction (2026-10-22; native ILP32):** PCSX2-independent native execution of the a30/a50 package-backed CE BSP transaction fixture initially faulted at `hcu_test_set_sound_camera_cluster()`. GDB located the fault in `assert_relocated_cluster_queries()`, not in `scenario_switch_structure_bsp()`: [`hcu_scenario_bsp_switch_main.c`](../tests/hcu_scenario_bsp_switch_main.c#L190) had declared and called the shared helper with two arguments, while the linked implementation in [`hcu_game_sound_host32.c`](../tests/hcu_game_sound_host32.c#L26) requires a third `real_point3d const *position`. Updated the prototype and every invocation to pass a stable zero camera position ([`hcu_scenario_bsp_switch_main.c:190-192,1208-1219,1257-1282,1737-1738`](../tests/hcu_scenario_bsp_switch_main.c#L190)). This removed the undefined ABI call and allowed the full focused retail transaction test to complete: `HALO_HCU_M2_PACKAGE_ROOT=/tmp/hcu-m2-capacity-repro.P5qdmI/packages HALO_HCU_M2_PACKAGE_MANIFEST=docs/reports/retail-cbsp-catalog-package-audit-e4a97218.json python3 -m pytest -q -rs tests/test_hcu_scenario_bsp_switch.py -k retail` → **1 passed, 2 map subtests passed** (native ILP32 outside the SIGSYS-restricted sandbox). This verifies existing transaction/failure coverage only; no new rollback fault class was added. Step 7 remains open for the wider CE-consumer, target-capacity and PS2 presentation gates; Step 8 remains open for production-profile memory, render/audio residency, match workload and hardware qualification.

**Step 7 sound-request seam characterization (2026-10-22; host ILP32; ISSUES):** [`tests/test_hcu_mp_sound_request.py`](../tests/test_hcu_mp_sound_request.py) compiles the real CE `sound_manager` path with a test-only recorder in front of the Xbox `queue_sound_to_channel` vtable and checks playback numbers against raw Xbox background-sound tags for all 13 stock MP maps. Production HALO_PS2 still sets platform `-1` ([`sound_preferences.c:34`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/sound/sound_preferences.c#L34)), so `sound_initialize` returns before selecting a platform, creating datums, or calling `initialize` ([`sound_manager.c:925`](../submodules/halo-ce-universal/source/sound/sound_manager.c#L925)); `platform_definitions[1]` stays NULL and no `port/ps2` file defines `sound_platform_definition`. Rerun: `python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_hcu_mp_sound_request.py` — **5 passed, 13 subtests**. All 13 maps have one background and one environment palette entry; 22 requests queued (Beaver Creek / Chill Out / Damnation use fog-plane cluster 0 because their fog `snde` index is NONE). Tag, permutation, and environment bytes stay unchanged; obstruction / `set_channel_location` / listener calls are 0. A 96-byte contract record × 73 virtual channels = 7,008 B and × 256 = 24,576 B, both inside the 524,288-byte `audio` region, but that region is the sample-cache budget and this run does not store requests there. Report [`soundreq/report.md`](../build/step7-swarm/soundreq/report.md), SHA-256 `67fd053f0d9b6fb4fdc5ccc31976d88b2b8471d3279ff7c2383456a30c530744`; test `a1b7167f2326ecb7b1b20cdedec07340b391424da42f27525c7b9d03c665c3ba`; bridge `472f3af8aede2002ef6bb4f0f5c4e34c540dd42049aeb46838cce914614b82e2`. This is host characterization only: not SPU2, not a port backend, not `scenario_get_sound_environment`, and not BSP publication. Do not implement a DirectSound or SPU2 seam from this slice. Step 7/8 stay open; arenas and the reserve are unchanged.

**Step 7 path-smoothing actor-backed PASS (2026-10-22; host ILP32):** the earlier ISSUES row was an oracle winding bug, not a CE path defect. Surface 108 is a planar triangle (n≈(0.327, 0.023, 0.945), no flip bit). Its directed ring is CCW in XY (shoelace +66.69). The returned point is the 3D barycenter on the plane (`d≈0`). CE `collision_surface_test_point2d(_z, TRUE)` rejects `cross>0` and therefore only accepts clockwise XY rings; the harness now prints that CW result as `CE_TEST2D … cw_z=0` and does not use it as the on-surface oracle. The consumer check uses plane distance plus the ring's own winding. Directed-walk vertex averaging replaced the old `vertex_indices[0]` center. `python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_hcu_mp_path_smoothing.py` — **1 passed**, 20 checks, 0 issues. Report [`pathsmooth/report.md`](../build/step7-swarm/pathsmooth/report.md), SHA-256 `3cdfde1f0219a489da40db12714d30c82835b0d5e317ab73db9d7903fad728e5`; test `beb66f462d481f91af06063c046fe9da3a55e082aa66746c8af95ec98e69d8dd`; host `c9a28c2bde4b9f9d71f6ce727678df13a11e0fce7e3b96312ac5b05aafa129a9`; ELF `81019f6092abcb747f5aa2b439553375b925291b7dd2424b5652204fb1b7dde6`. Still host-only, Blood Gulch only, synthetic Step 8 elite, no live steering. Do not fill hollow `actv` tags. Step 7/8 stay open; arenas and the reserve are unchanged.

**Step 7 sound-request seam stays ISSUES / M5 (2026-10-22):** the host recorder already showed 22 raw-palette background requests across all 13 maps. Production HALO_PS2 still sets platform `-1` and `sound_initialize` returns before any platform queue. Closing that requires a bounded PS2 `sound_platform_definition` that stages CE requests in the 524,288-byte `audio` region, then SPU2 playback. [`plans/5.hcu-remake.md`](5.hcu-remake.md) assigns that adapter to M5, and `sound_preferences.c` already calls it a later migration slice. Implementing DirectSound or inventing an SPU2 backend here would leave Step 7/8 scope. Leave platform `-1`. Step 7/8 stay open.

**Blood Gulch parent-viewer PCSX2 pixels (2026-10-22; not HCU admission):** rebuilt the conservative halo-ps2 world viewer after dirty parent sources invalidated `build/world-elf/result.json`, then ran PCSX2 2.8.2 recompiler against the existing m10-static-sky Blood Gulch packs. Official `tools/run_world_viewer.py` still requires the missing `build/platform-elf/platform.elf` M06 pin, so this launch reused that runner with `require_platform` skipped. Guest loaded `bloodgulch.hp2` BSP0 (79 materials, 396 batches, 5,503 tris, 30 clusters) plus TEXS/LM0, presented 181 frames at the valley camera `65,-95,9 / -70,-5`, and dumped a 512×448 PSMCT16S readback at frame 120. Final profiler: 3,270 tris, 221 batches, 42 materials, clusters 19/30, 0 texture fails/deferrals, 0 chain overflow, 0 dropped frames; level arena 1,348 / 12,288 KB. Run [`world-recompiler-2258c8ca7e924518bfe1d882e473f354`](../build/emulator/runs/world-recompiler-2258c8ca7e924518bfe1d882e473f354); ELF SHA-256 `8e3df9100218bf636639415e4b60db71d206dd598a44397906aea2c4d36c245e`; result/log SHA-256 `9ae55e8e6f667cdcd779df30833cd821795cc44d0792c6c44aef0afaddd1543a` / `4ecf5074050ca2b6367c9be1eddc67e733b5de8da16cae4c8102b60a25066db5`; GS raw/PNG SHA-256 `b80fea8f82ea0fca6a9f9adf3503d7211492c261433efc244806cc779c85fc6e` / `02300f664fd614a6920daaab36cb1dc2fa5a2389656d7611c4f2f467f6b74d59`. This is the standalone parent viewer, not the HCU+parent combined machine: no CE snapshot consumer, no gameplay-map admission, no interpreter pair, no objects/sky, and no Step 7/8 or M3 close.
